<sup>26</sup> Daily he is compassionate and lendeth, And his seed is for blessing.

<sup>27</sup> Depart from evil and do good, And dwell for ever.

<!-- PAGE 892 -->

<sup>28</sup> For Jehovah loveth judgment, And doth not forsake his meek ones; For ever shall they be preserved: And the seed of the ungodly shall be cut off.

<sup>29</sup> The righteous shall inherit the earth, And shall dwell for ever upon it.

<sup>30</sup> The mouth of the righteous shall speak wisdom;

<sup>31</sup> The law of his God is in his heart; Slide not shall his steps.

<sup>32</sup> Watch doth the ungodly the righteous man, And seeketh to put him to death.

<sup>33</sup> Jehovah will not leave him in his hand, Nor condemn him when he is judged.

<sup>34</sup> Wait for Jehovah, and keep his way, And he shall exalt thee to inherit the earth: When the ungodly shall be cut off thou shalt see it.

<sup>35</sup> I have seen the ungodly man robust, <span style="color:#800000">ff76</span> And spreading himself like a green bay tree;

<sup>36</sup> And he passed away, and to I he was not; And I sought for his place, and he was not found.

<sup>37</sup> Mark the perfect man, and consider the upright; For the end of such a man is peace.

<sup>38</sup> But transgressors shall be destroyed together: The end of the ungodly shall be cut off.

<sup>39</sup> But the salvation of the righteous is from Jehovah: *He will* be their strength in the time of trouble.

<sup>40</sup> And help them shall Jehovah, and deliver them: He shall deliver them from the wicked: He shall save them, because they trust in him.

<!-- PAGE 893 -->

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 38</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*A Psalm of David: to bring to remembrance*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> O Jehovah! do not in thy fury rebuke me, And in thy wrath do not chasten me.

<sup>2</sup> For thy arrows go down in me, And descend upon me doth thy hand.

<sup>3</sup> There is no soundness in my flesh, because of thy wrath, Nor peace in my bones because of my sin.

<sup>4</sup> For my iniquities have passed over my head, Like a burden that is weighty, they are too heavy for me.

<sup>5</sup> Putrid [*and*] corrupt have my wounds become, Because of my foolishness.

<sup>6</sup> I am bowed down, I am brought low exceedingly; Day by day a mourner I do walk:

<sup>7</sup> For my reins are filled with a burning heat; Nor is there soundness in my flesh.

<sup>8</sup> I am enfeebled and broken exceedingly: I have roared through the disquietude of my heart.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>9</sup> O Lord! before thee is all my desire, And my groaning from thee is not hidden,</p>

<sup>10</sup> My heart hath throbbed, forsaken me hath my strength; And the light of my eyes, it also is gone from me.

<sup>11</sup> My friends and my companions aloof from my stroke have stood, And my kinsmen afar off have stood.

<sup>12</sup> And they have laid snares who sought for my soul; Arid those who sought my hurt have threatened mischiefs; And deceits daily do they meditate.

<sup>13</sup> But as for me, like a deaf man I hear not; And *I am* like a dumb man who openeth not his mouth.

<!-- PAGE 894 -->

<sup>14</sup> And I was as a man who doth not hear, And in whose mouth there are no reproofs.

<sup>15</sup> For on thee, O Jehovah! do I wait: Thou wilt answer *me*, O Lord, my God!

<sup>16</sup> For I said, “Lest they should rejoice over me: “ At the slipping of my foot against me they magnified themselves, <sup>17</sup> Surely as for me I am ready to halt; And my sorrow is before me continually.

<sup>18</sup> Surely my iniquity I declare; I am in consternation because of my sin.

<sup>19</sup> But my enemies are living; <span style="color:#800000">ff77</span> they are strong; And those are become mighty who hate me wrongfully.

<sup>20</sup> And they who requite me evil for good are against me, Because I follow [*what is*] good.

<sup>21</sup> Forsake me not, O Jehovah, my God! Be not far from me.

<sup>22</sup> Hasten to my help, O Lord, my salvation!

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 39</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician, Jeduthun. A Psalm of David*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> I said “I will take heed to my ways, “That I sin not with my tongue: “I will keep my mouth with a muzzle, “While the ungodly man standeth before me.”

<sup>2</sup> I was dumb not uttering a word; I held my peace [*even*] from good; And my sorrow was stirred.

<sup>3</sup> My heart became hot within me; In my musing a fire did burn; I spake with my tongue.

<sup>4</sup> Cause me to know, O Jehovah! my end, And the number of my days, That I may know of what duration I am.

<!-- PAGE 895 -->

<sup>5</sup> Behold! as an handbreadth thou hast made my days, And my duration is as nothing before thee: Surely altogether vanity is every mart while he standeth.

<sup>6</sup> Surely in a shadow man walketh; [Selah. Surely in vain is he disquieted: They heap together, and know not who shall gather.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>7</sup> And now what do I wait for, O Lord? My hope is towards thee.</p>

<sup>8</sup> From all my sins deliver thou me: The reproach of the foolish make me not.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>9</sup> I was dumb; I will not open my mouth, Because thou hast done it.</p>

<sup>10</sup> Remove from me thy stroke: By the blow of thy hand I have failed.

<sup>11</sup> With rebukes for iniquity thou chastisest man; And causest to waste away as a moth his beauty: Surely vanity *is* every man. Selah.

<sup>12</sup> Hear my prayer, O Jehovah! And to my cry give ear; At my tears be not silent; For a stranger am I before thee, A sojourner like all my fathers.

<sup>13</sup> Let me alone, that I may recover strength, Before I depart and be no more.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 40</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician. A Psalm of David*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> I have patiently waited for Jehovah, And he inclined to me, and heard my cry.

<sup>2</sup> And he drew me out of the roaring pit, Out of the mire of clay, And set upon a rock my feet; He hath established my steps.

<!-- PAGE 896 -->

<sup>3</sup> And he hath put in my mouth a song [that ισ new, *Even* praise to our God: See shall many and shall fear, And shall trust in Jehovah.

<sup>4</sup> Blessed is the man who maketh Jehovah his confidence, And hath not respected the proud, Nor those who turn aside to lying.

<sup>5</sup> Many, O Jehovah, my God! are thy wonderful works which thou hast done; And thy thoughts towards us it is impossible to reckon up in order to thee: I will declare and speak [*of them;*] [Βυτ they are more than can be told.

<sup>6</sup> Sacrifice and oblation thou hast not taken pleasure in: *But* my ears hast thou made fit:  <span style="color:#800000">ff78</span> Burnt-offering and sin-offering thou hast not required.

<sup>7</sup> Then I said, “Behold! I come; “In the volume of the book it is written of me, <sup>8</sup> “That I may do thy pleasure, O my God! “I have desired *to do it*, “And thy law is in the midst of my bowels.”

<sup>9</sup> I have proclaimed thy righteousness in the assembly great: Behold my lips I will not restrain; O Jehovah! thou knowest it.

<sup>10</sup> Thy righteousness I have not hidden within my heart; Thy truth and thy salvation I have declared: I have not concealed thy goodness and thy truth in the assembly great.

<sup>11</sup> O thou, Jehovah! withhold not thy compassions from me; Let thy goodness and thy truth continually preserve me.

<sup>12</sup> For encompassed me have evils innumerable; Laid hold upon me have my iniquities, *yea in so great number* that I cannot see them: They are more in number than the hairs of my head; *And* my heart hath failed me.

<sup>13</sup> Let it please thee, O Jehovah! to deliver me; O Jehovah! to my aid make haste.

<!-- PAGE 897 -->

<sup>14</sup> Let them be put to shame and confounded together Who seek after my soul to destroy it; Let them be turned backward and put to shame Who seek after my hurt.

<sup>15</sup> Let them be destroyed for a reward of their shame, Who have said to me, “Aha! aha!”

<sup>16</sup> Let all those exult and rejoice in thee who seek thee; And let those say continually, “May Jehovah be magnified” — “Those who love thy salvation.

<sup>17</sup> But as for me *I am* poor and needy. Jehovah hath regarded me; My help and my deliverer *art thou*: O thou, my God! delay not.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 41</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician. A Psalm of David*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Blessed is he who judgeth wisely of the poor: In the day of evil Jehovah will deliver him.

<sup>2</sup> Jehovah will keep him, and preserve him alive: He shall be blessed upon the earth: And thou will not give him up to the desire of his enemies.

<sup>3</sup> Jehovah will support him upon the bed of sorrow: All his bed thou hast turned in his sickness.

<sup>4</sup> I have said, “O Jehovah! have mercy upon me; “Heal my soul, for I have sinned against thee.”

<!-- PAGE 898 -->
<sup>5</sup> My enemies have spoken evil of me, “When will he die, and perish shall his name? “ <sup>6</sup> And if he come to see me falsely doth he speak; His heart gathereth iniquity to itself’: When he goeth abroad he speaketh [*it*.] <sup>7</sup> Together against me do all my haters whisper; Against me do they devise evil for me, [*saying*,] <sup>8</sup> “An evil deed of Belial cleaveth fast to him; “And he who lieth shall not rise again.”

<sup>9</sup> Even the man of my peace in whom I trusted, Who eateth of my bread, hath lifted up against me the heel.

<sup>10</sup> Do thou *then*, O Jehovah! have mercy upon me; Raise me up, and I will recompense them.

<sup>11</sup> By this I have known that I have been acceptable to thee, Because my enemy shall not triumph over me.

<sup>12</sup> And as for me, in my integrity thou wilt uphold me, And wilt establish me before thy face for ever.

<sup>13</sup> Blessed *be* Jehovah, the God of Israel, For ever and ever. Amen and Amen.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1">PART SECOND.<sup>79</sup></p>

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 42</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician. A lesson of instruction to the Sons of Korah*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> As the hart crieth for the fountains of waters, So my soul crieth for thee, O God!

<sup>2</sup> Thirsted hath my soul for God, for the living God: When shall I come to appear before the face of God?

<sup>3</sup> To me have my tears been *for* bread, by day and night, While it is said to me daily, “Where is thy God? “ <sup>4</sup> When these things I remember, I pour out within me my soul, Because I had gone with the procession, Leading them even to the house of God, With the voice of exultation and praise, — The multitude dancing for joy.

<sup>5</sup> ‘Why art thou cast down, O my soul! And why art thou disquieted within me? Wait thou for God; for I shall yet give him thanks, [*For*] the salvations of his countenance.

<!-- PAGE 899 -->

<sup>6</sup> O my God! my soul within me is cast down, When I remember thee from the land of Jordan and of Hermonim, From the mountain Mizar.

<sup>7</sup> Deep unto deep calleth at the noise of thy waterspouts: All thy waves and all thy billows over me have gone.

<sup>8</sup> By day command will Jehovah his loving-kindness, And by night his song *shall be* with me, And prayer to the God of my life.

<sup>9</sup> I will say to God, my Rock, “Why hast thou forgotten me? “Why mourning do I go because of the oppression of *my* enemy?”

<sup>10</sup> [*It is as*] a wound in my bones when my enemies reproach me, Saying to me daily, “Where is thy God? “ <sup>11</sup> ‘Why art thou cast down, O my soul? And why art thou disquieted within me? Hope in God; for I shall yet give thanks to him, The salvations of my countenance, and my God.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 43</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Judge me, O God! and plead my cause: From a people without mercy, from a man deceitful and wicked deliver me.

<sup>2</sup> For thou *art* the God of my strength; Why art thou estranged from me? Why mourning do I go because of the oppression of the enemy?

<sup>3</sup> Send forth thy light and thy truth; Let them direct me, let them conduct me, To the mountain of thy holiness, and to thy tabernacles.

<sup>4</sup> And I will go to the altar of God, To the God of the joy of my rejoicing; And I will celebrate thee upon the harp, O God, my God!

<!-- PAGE 900 -->

<sup>5</sup> Why art thou cast down, O my soul! And why art thou disquieted within me? Hope in God, for I shall yet celebrate him, *Who is* the salvation of my countenance and my God.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 44</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician. Of the sons of Korah. Giving instruction*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> O God! with our ears we have heard, Our fathers have told us, The work which thou hast done in their days, In the days of old.

<sup>2</sup> Thou with thy hand hast expelled the heathen, and planted them: Thou hast wasted the peoples and multiplied them:

<sup>3</sup> For not by their own sword acquired they the land, And their own arm did not save them; But thy right hand and thy arm, and the light of thy countenance, Because thou wast favorable to them.

<sup>4</sup> Thou, even thou, *art* my King; O God! command salvations for Jacob.

<sup>5</sup> Through thee our adversaries with the horn we have pushed; In thy name we have trampled under foot those who rise up against us.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>6</sup> For not in my bow will I trust, And my sword will not save me.</p>

<sup>7</sup> Surely thou hast saved us from our enemies, And our haters thou hast put to shame.

<sup>8</sup> In God we will boast all the day, And thy name for ever shall we praise. Selah.

<sup>9</sup> Nevertheless thou hast abhorred us and put us to shame, And thou goest not forth *any more* with our armies.

<sup>10</sup> Thou hast made us to turn back from him that afflicteth us, And our haters have spoiled *us* for themselves.

<!-- PAGE 901 -->

<sup>11</sup> Thou hast given us as sheep for food; And among the heathen thou hast scattered us.

<sup>12</sup> Thou hast sold thy people, and not become rich, And thou hast not increased the price of them.

<sup>13</sup> Thou hast made us a reproach to our neighbors, A scorn and derision to those who are round about us.

<sup>14</sup> Thou hast made us a byword among the heathen, A shaking of the head among the peoples.

<sup>15</sup> Daily *is* my reproach before me, And the shame of my face hath *quite* covered me, <sup>16</sup> Because of the voice of him who reproacheth and revileth, Because of the face of the adversary and avenger.

<sup>17</sup> All this is come upon us, and we have not forgotten thee, Nor dealt perfidiously in thy covenant;

<sup>18</sup> Not turned back hath our heart, Nor declined have our steps from thy path.

<sup>19</sup> Although thou hast crushed us in the place of dragons,  And covered us with the shadow of death.

<sup>20</sup> If we have forgotten the name of our God, And have spread forth our hands to a God that is strange;

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>21</sup> Shall not God search out this? For he knoweth the secrets of the heart.</p>

<sup>22</sup> Surely for thy sake we are killed daily; We are accounted as sheep [*appointed*] for slaughter.

<sup>23</sup> Arise, why sleepest thou, O Lord? Awake, do not forget *us* for ever.

<sup>24</sup> Wherefore thy face dost thou hide? Wilt thou forget our misery and our affliction?

<!-- PAGE 902 -->

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>25</sup> For humbled to the dust is our soul; Cleave to the earth doth our belly.</p>

<sup>26</sup> Arise for our help, And redeem us for the sake of thy lovingkindness.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 45</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician. Upon the lilies. Of the sons of Korah. Giving*</span> <span style="color:#000080">*instruction. A Song of loves*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Boiling over is my heart to speak a goodly theme, Speak shall I myself of my works concerning the King: My tongue is as the pen of a swift writer.

<sup>2</sup> Thou art fair above the sons of men; Diffused is grace on thy lips; Because God hath blessed thee for ever.

<sup>3</sup> Gird thy sword upon thy thigh, O mighty one! With majesty and glory.

<sup>4</sup> And in thy majesty prosper thou; Ride forth upon the word of truth, and meekness and righteousness; And thy right hand shall teach thee terrible things:

<sup>5</sup> Thy arrows *are* sharp (so that the people fall under thee) In the hearts of the enemies of the king.

<sup>6</sup> Thy throne, O God! *is* for ever and ever; The scepter of rectitude *is* the scepter of thy kingdom.

<sup>7</sup> Thou lovest righteousness and hatest impiety; Because anointed thee hath God, thy God, With the oil of gladness above thy fellows.

<sup>8</sup> Myrrh, and aloes and cassia [*are*] *in* all thy garments, Out of the ivory palaces, whence they have made thee glad.

<sup>9</sup> The daughters of kings [*were*] among thy maids of honor; Stand did thy consort at thy right hand in gold of Ophir.

<!-- PAGE 903 -->

<sup>10</sup> Hearken, O daughter! and consider and incline thy ear; And forget thy people, and the house of thy father.

<sup>11</sup> And enamored shall be the King with thy beauty; For he *is* thy Lord, and thou shalt worship him.

<sup>12</sup> And the daughter of Tyre [*shall come*] with a present; Thy favor entreat shall the rich among the people.

<sup>13</sup> All glorious is the daughter of the King within; Of garments embroidered with gold *is* her clothing.

<sup>14</sup> In raiment of needle-work she shall be brought to the King; Her virgins that follow her, her companions shall be brought to thee.

<sup>15</sup> They shall be brought with joy and exultation; They shall enter into the palace of the King.

<sup>16</sup> Instead of thy fathers shall be thy children; Thou shalt make them princes in all the earth.

<sup>17</sup> I will make thy name to be remembered throughout all generation; Therefore celebrate thee shall the peoples for ever and ever.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 46</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician. Of the sons of Korah. Upon Alamoth. A Song*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> God is to us a protection and strength; A help in troubles he is found exceedingly.

<sup>2</sup> Therefore we will not fear when moved shall be the earth, And fall shall the mountains into the heart of the sea.

<sup>3</sup> [*When*] roar and rage tempestuously shall the waters thereof; [*When*] shake shall the mountains with the swelling thereof. Selah.

<sup>4</sup> The streams of her river shall make glad the city of God, The Sanctuary of the tabernacles of the Most High.

<!-- PAGE 904 -->

<sup>5</sup> God *is* in the midst of her; she shall not be moved: Help her will God at the dawn of the morning.

<sup>6</sup> Rage did the peoples, moved were the kingdoms: He uttered his voice, melt did the earth.

<sup>7</sup> Jehovah of armies *is* with us; A fortress for us *is* the God of Jacob. Selah.

<sup>8</sup> Come ye, consider the works of Jehovah, What desolations he hath wrought in the earth.

<sup>9</sup> He maketh to cease battles, even to the ends of the earth; He breaketh the bow, he shattereth in pieces the arms; The chariot he burneth with fire.

<sup>10</sup> Be still and know that I am God: I will be exalted among the heathen; I will be exalted in the earth.

<sup>11</sup> Jehovah of armies is with us; A fortress for us *is* the God of Jacob. Selah.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 47</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician. Of the sons of Korah. Upon Alamoth. A Song*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> O all ye peoples! clap the hand; Shout unto God with the voice of exultation;

<sup>2</sup> For Jehovah *is* high, terrible, A great king over all the earth.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>3</sup> He hath put in order the people under us, And the nations under our feet.</p>

<sup>4</sup> He hath chosen for us our inheritance, The glory of Jacob, whom he hath loved. Selah.

<sup>5</sup> Gone up is God with a shout, Jehovah with the sound of a trumpet.

<sup>6</sup> Sing psalms to God, sing psalms; Sing psalms to our King, sing psalms;

<!-- PAGE 905 -->

<sup>7</sup> For king of all the earth is God; Sing praises, all ye who understand!

<sup>8</sup> The kingdom he hath obtained over the heathen; God sitteth upon the throne of his holiness.

<sup>9</sup> The princes of the peoples are assembled together To the people of the God of Abraham: For to God belong the shields of the earth: He is greatly exalted.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 48</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*A Song of a Psalm. Of the sons of Korah*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Great is Jehovah and to be praised greatly In the city of our God, in the mountain of his holiness.

<sup>2</sup> Beautiful for situation, the joy of the whole earth, The mountain of Zion, on the sides of the north, The city of the great King.

<sup>3</sup> God in her palaces is known for a defense.

<sup>4</sup> For behold! the kings assembled, They passed away together.

<sup>5</sup> They themselves saw, so they marveled; They were frightened, they fled precipitately, <sup>6</sup> Fear seized upon them there, Pain as of one in travail.

<sup>7</sup> By the east wind thou breakest in pieces the ships of Tarshish.

<sup>8</sup> As we have heard, so have we seen In the city of Jehovah of Hosts, In the city of our God: God will establish it for ever. Selah <sup>9</sup> We have waited, O God! for thy mercy In the midst of thy temple, <sup>10</sup> As *is* thy name, O God! So *is* thy praise unto the ends of the earth: Of righteousness full is thy right hand.

<!-- PAGE 906 -->

<sup>11</sup> Rejoice shall Mount Zion, Exult shall the daughters of Judah, Because of thy judgments.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>12</sup> Encompass Zion, and walk round about her. Number the towers thereof,</p>

<sup>13</sup> Set your heart to her walls, Exalt her towers, That ye may make report to the generations to come.

<sup>14</sup> For this God is our God for ever and ever: He will be our guide even unto death.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 49</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician. Of the sons of Korah. A Psalm*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Hear this all ye peoples; Give ear all ye inhabitants of the world:

<sup>2</sup> Both ye sons of Adam, and ye sons of men, Rich and poor together.

<sup>3</sup> My mouth shall speak of wisdoms, And the meditation of my heart is of understanding.

<sup>4</sup> I will incline to a parable my ear: I will open upon the harp my enigma.

<sup>5</sup> Wherefore should I fear in the days of evil? The iniquity of my heel shall compass me about.

<sup>6</sup> They trust in their wealth, And in the multitude of their riches do glory.

<sup>7</sup> The brother shall not be able to redeem; None shall give to God the price of his redemption.

<sup>8</sup> And precious shall be the redemption of their soul, And *their* continuance for ever:

<sup>9</sup> That he should still live for ever, And not see the grave.

<!-- PAGE 907 -->

<sup>10</sup> For he shall see that wise men die, Together the fool and the brutish person shall perish, And leave to strangers their wealth.

<sup>11</sup> Their inward thought is [*how to make*] their houses [*to continue*] for ever, Their dwelling-places from generation to generation: They have called out their names upon the earth.

<sup>12</sup> And man in honor shall not abide; He hath become like the beasts: they perish.

<sup>13</sup> This their way *is* foolishness in them, And their posterity in their saying will acquiesce. Selah.

<sup>14</sup> Like sleep in the grave they are laid; Death shall feed them; And have dominion over them shall the upright in the morning, And their strength shall wax old; The grave *shall receive* them from their dwelling.

<sup>15</sup> But God shall redeem my soul from the hand of the grave; For he hath taken me up. Selah.

<sup>16</sup> Be not thou afraid when a man shall become rich, When increased shall be the glory of his house;

<sup>17</sup> For he shall not at his death carry all away: Descend not after him shall his glory:

<sup>18</sup> For his soul in his lifetime he will bless, And they shall praise thee when thou doest well to thyself.

<sup>19</sup> He shall come to the age of his fathers, Even for ever he shall not see the light.

<sup>20</sup> Man *is* in honor, and will not understand: He hath become like the beasts: they shall perish.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 50</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*A Song, of Asaph*</span></p>

<!-- PAGE 908 -->

<sup>1</sup> The God of gods, Jehovah hath spoken, And called the earth From the rising of the sun unto the going down thereof.

<sup>2</sup> Out of Zion, the Perfection of beauty, God hath shined.

<sup>3</sup> Come shall our God, and shall not keep silence; A fire before him shall devour, And round about him a tempest shall rage exceedingly.

<sup>4</sup> He shall call to the heavens from above, And to the earth, to judge his people.

<sup>5</sup> “Gather together to me my meek ones, (*will he say*),  “Those who strike a covenant with me over sacrifices.”

<sup>6</sup> And declare shall the heavens his righteousness: For God is judge himself. Selah.

<sup>7</sup> “Hear, O my people! and I will speak; “O Israel! and I will announce to thee:  “God, [*even*] thy God am I.

<sup>8</sup> “Not for thy sacrifices will I reprove thee; “And thy burntofferings *are* before me continually.

<sup>9</sup> “I will not take out of thy house a calf, “*Nor* out of thy folds he-goats:

<sup>10</sup> “For mine are all the beasts of the forest, “The cattle upon a thousand hills.

<sup>11</sup> “I know all the birds of the mountains; “And the wild beasts of the field are at my command.

<sup>12</sup> “If I am hungry, I will not tell thee: “For mine is the world and the fullness thereof.

<sup>13</sup> “Will I eat the flesh of bulls, “And the blood of goats will I drink?

<sup>14</sup> “Sacrifice unto God praise, “And pay unto the Most High thy vows.

<!-- PAGE 909 -->

<sup>15</sup> “Call upon me in the day of trouble; “I will deliver thee, and thou shalt glorify me.

<sup>16</sup> “But unto the wicked said hath God, “What hast thou to do to declare my statutes, “[*Or*] that thou shouldest take my covenant into thy lips?

<sup>17</sup> “Also thou hatest correction, “And castest my words behind thee.

<sup>18</sup> “If thou seest a thief, thou wilt run with him, “And with adulterers is thy portion.

<sup>19</sup> “Thy mouth thou puttest forth to evil, “And thy tongue frameth deceit, <sup>20</sup> “Thou wilt sit against thy brother; “Thou wilt speak against the sons of thy mother; “Thou wilt set forth slander.

<sup>21</sup> “These things thou hast done, and I kept silence; “Thou thoughtest that I would be like thyself’: “I will reprove thee, and set them in order before thine eyes.

<sup>22</sup> “Now consider this, ye who forget God, “Lest I seize upon you and there be none to deliver.

<sup>23</sup> “He who sacrificeth praise will glorify me: “And he who ordereth his way to him will I show the salvation of God.”

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 51</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician. A Psalm of David, when Xathan the prophet came to*</span> <span style="color:#000080">*him, after he had gone in to Bathsheba*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Have pity upon me, O God! according to thy mercy; According to the multitude of thy compassions blot out my iniquities.

<sup>2</sup> Multiply to wash me from my sin, And from my wickedness do thou cleanse me.

<sup>3</sup> For my sins I know, And my wickedness is before me continually.

<!-- PAGE 910 -->

<sup>4</sup> Against thee, against thee only, have I sinned, And that which was displeasing in thy sight have I done; That thou mayest be justified when thou speakest, And be pure in giving judgment.

<sup>5</sup> Behold in iniquity I was born, And in sin conceive me did my mother.

<sup>6</sup> Behold, truth thou hast loved in the inmost parts, And in secret, wisdom thou hast made me know.

<sup>7</sup> Thou shalt purge me with hyssop, and I shall be clean; Thou shalt wash me, and I shall be whiter than snow.

<sup>8</sup> Make me to hear joy and gladness; And exult shall the bones which thou hast broken.

<sup>9</sup> Hide thy face from my sins, And all my iniquities blot out.

<sup>10</sup> A heart [*that is*] clean create in me, O God! And a spirit [*that is*] right renew in my inward parts.

<sup>11</sup> Cast me not away from thy face, And the Spirit of thy holiness take not from me.

<sup>12</sup> Restore to me the joy of thy salvation, And with a free spirit uphold me.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>13</sup> I will teach transgressors thy ways, the ungodly to time shall be converted.</p>

<sup>14</sup> Deliver me from bloods, O God! O God of my salvation! And sing aloud shall my tongue of thy righteousness.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>15</sup> O Lord! my lips do thou open, And my mouth shall show forth thy praise.</p>

<sup>16</sup> For thou wilt not accept a sacrifice; Though I should give a burnt-offering, it would not please thee.

<sup>17</sup> The sacrifices of God are an afflicted spirit: A heart afflicted and contrite, O God! thou wilt not despise.

<!-- PAGE 911 -->

<sup>18</sup> Do good in thy good pleasure to Zion; Build thou the walls of Jerusalem.

<sup>19</sup> Then shalt thou accept the sacrifices of righteousness, The burnt-offering and oblation; <span style="color:#800000">ff80</span> Then shall come upon thy altar calves.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 52</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician. A lesson of instruction of David: when Doeg the*</span> <span style="color:#000080">*Edomite came and told Saul, and said to him, that David had come into the*</span> <span style="color:#000080">*house of Abimelech*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Why dost thou glory in thy wickedness, O mighty man? The goodness of God *continueth* daily.

<sup>2</sup> Thy tongue reckoneth up mischiefs, Like a razor [*that is*] sharp working deceitfully.

<sup>3</sup> Thou lovest wickedness more than goodness; Falsehood more than to speak righteousness. Selah.

<sup>4</sup> Thou lovest all words of deceit, O thou tongue of guilefulness!

<sup>5</sup> Likewise God shall destroy thee for ever: He shall lay hold on thee and pluck thee out of thy tabernacle, And root thee out of the land of the living. Selah.

<sup>6</sup> See it also shall the righteous ones, and shall fear, And at him they shall laugh.

<sup>7</sup> Behold! the man who made not God his strength; And trusted in the abundance of his riches, And was strong in his wickedness.

<sup>8</sup> But as for me *I shall be* like an olive-tree [*that is*] green in the house of God: I have trusted in the goodness of God for ever and ever.

<sup>9</sup> I will celebrate thee for ever, because thou hast done [*it*:]  <span style="color:#800000">ff81</span> I will wait on thy name, For it is good in the presence of thy meek ones.

<!-- PAGE 912 -->

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 53</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician, upon Mahalath. A lesson of instruction ofDavid*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> The fool hath said in his heart, “There is not a God:” They have corrupted [*all good order*,] they have done abominable work: There is none who doeth good.

<sup>2</sup> God from heaven looked down upon the sons of men, To see if there was any who did understand, Who did seek after God.

<sup>3</sup> Every one of them hath gone back; They have all together become corrupt: There is none that doeth good, not even one.

<sup>4</sup> Have they no knowledge, all *these* workers of iniquity? Eating my people [*as*] they eat bread: Upon Jehovah they have not called.

<sup>5</sup> There were they afraid with fear, where there was not fear; For scattered hath Jehovah the bones of him that encampeth against thee: Thou hast put them to shame because God hath despised them.

<sup>6</sup> Who shall give out of Zion deliverance to Israel? When bring back shall God the captivity of his people, Rejoice shall Jacob, exult shall Israel.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 54</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician, on Neginoth. A lesson of instruction of David. When*</span> <span style="color:#000080">*the Ziphims came and said to Saul, “Doth not David hide himself with us?”*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> O God! by thy name save me, And by thy strength judge me.

<sup>2</sup> O God! hear my prayer, Give ear to the words of my mouth.

<sup>3</sup> For strangers have risen up against me, And terrible ones have sought after my soul: They have not set God before them. Selah.

<!-- PAGE 913 -->

<sup>4</sup> Behold! God is a helper to me; The Lord is with those who uphold my soul.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>5</sup> He shall repay evil to my adversaries: In thy truth cut them off.</p>

<sup>6</sup> Willingly will I sacrifice to thee; I will celebrate thy name, O Jehovah! for it is good.

<sup>7</sup> For out of all trouble he hath delivered me; And [*punishment*] upon my adversaries mine eye hath seen.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 55</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician on Neginoth. A lesson of instruction of David*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Give ear, O God! to my prayer, And hide not thyself from my supplication.

<sup>2</sup> Attend to me, and answer me: I will wail in my address, and will become tumultuous.

<sup>3</sup> By reason of the voice of the enemy — Under the oppression of the ungodly; For they cast upon me iniquity, In wrath they set themselves against me.

<sup>4</sup> My heart trembleth within me, And the terrors of death have fallen upon me.

<sup>5</sup> Fear and trembling are come upon me, And horror hath overwhelmed me.

<sup>6</sup> And I said, “Who will give me wings like a dove? “I will fly away and be at rest.

<sup>7</sup> “Lo! I will flee far away, “I will repose in the desert. Selah.

<sup>8</sup> “I will hasten my escape from the tempestuous whirlwind.”

<sup>9</sup> Destroy, O Lord! divide their tongue: For I have seen oppression and strife in the city.

<!-- PAGE 914 -->

<sup>10</sup> By day and night they go round it upon the walls thereof: And labor and sorrow are in the midst of it.

<sup>11</sup> Wickedness  <span style="color:#800000">ff82</span> is in the midst of it; And fraud and deceit depart not from the streets thereof.

<sup>12</sup> Truly it was not an enemy who reproached me, For [*then*] I could have borne [*it*:] *It was* not an adversary who magnified himself against me, For [*then*] I would have hid himself from him.

<sup>13</sup> But thou, a man according to my own rank, My guide, and my familiar friend.

<sup>14</sup> We sweetly exchanged our secret thoughts; Into the house of God we walked in company.

<sup>15</sup> Let death seize upon them, Let them descend into the grave alive; For wickedness  <span style="color:#800000">ff83</span> *is* I, their dwelling, in the midst of them.

<sup>16</sup> As for me, to God; will I cry, And Jehovah shall save me.

<sup>17</sup> In the evening, and the morning, and at noonday Will I pray, and cry aloud; And he shall hear my voice.

<sup>18</sup> He hath redeemed into peace my soul From the battle which was against me: For many were with me.

<sup>19</sup> Hear shall God, and shall afflict them, Even he who sitteth from ancient time. Selah. Because they have no changes, And fear not God.

<sup>20</sup> He hath set his hands against those who were at peace with him: He hath broken his covenant.

<sup>21</sup> Smoother than butter are the words of his mouth, And *in* his heart *is* war; Softer are his words than oil, And [*yet*] they *are* darts.

<!-- PAGE 915 -->

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>22</sup> Cast upon Jehovah whatever blessings thou askest from him, <span style="color:#800000">ff84</span></p>

And he shall feed thee; He shall not suffer for ever the righteous man to stagger.

<sup>23</sup> Thou, O God! shalt cast them into the pit of corruption: The men of blood and deceit shall not live out half their days: But as for me, I will hope in thee.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 56</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician upon, the silent dove in distant places. Michtam of*</span> <span style="color:#000080">*David, when the Philistines took him in Gath*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Have mercy upon me, O God, For swallow me up doth man; He daily assaulting doth oppress me.

<sup>2</sup> Swallow me up do *my* enemies daily: Truly many *strive* to oppress me, *O Jehovah*, Most High!

<sup>3</sup> In the day when I was afraid, I in thee did trust.

<sup>4</sup> In God I will praise his word; In God have I trusted; I will not fear what flesh can do to me.

<sup>5</sup> Every day my words disquiet me; Against me *are* all their thoughts for evil.

<sup>6</sup> They assemble together, they hide themselves, My heels they watch, Because they desire my soul.

<sup>7</sup> In *their* iniquity *they think* there is escape for them: In *thy* wrath the peoples thou wilt cast down, O God!

<sup>8</sup> My wanderings thou numbereth, even thou: Put my tears into thy bottle; Are they not in thy register?

<sup>9</sup> Then my enemies shall be turned back in the day when I cry: This I know, because God is with me.

<sup>10</sup> In God will I praise [*his*] word; In Jehovah will I praise [*his*] word.

<!-- PAGE 916 -->

<sup>11</sup> In God have I trusted; I will not fear what man can do to me.

<sup>12</sup> Upon me, O God! are thy vows, I will render praises to thee.

<sup>13</sup> For thou hast delivered my soul from death: Hast thou not *also delivered* my feet from falling? That I may walk before God in the light of the living.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 57</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician. Destroy not. Michtam of David. When he fled from*</span> <span style="color:#000080">*the face of Saul in the cave*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Have mercy upon me, O God! have mercy upon me; For in thee my soul doth trust; And in the shadow of thy wings will I hope, Until iniquity pass away.

<sup>2</sup> I will cry to God Most High, Who perfecteth [*his work*] towards me.

<sup>3</sup> He shall send from Heaven, And shall save me from the reproach of him who swalloweth me up: Send forth shall God his mercy and his truth.

<sup>4</sup> My soul is in the midst of lions; I lie among those who are set on fire, — Among the sons of men, whose teeth are spears and arrows, And their tongue a sword [*that is*] sharp.

<sup>5</sup> Be thou exalted above the heavens, O God! Above all the earth thy glory.

<sup>6</sup> A net they have prepared for my steps: Bowed down is my soul: They have digged before me a pit, *But* they have fallen into it. Selah <sup>7</sup> Prepared *is* my heart, O God! prepared *is* my heart: I will sing, and chant psalms.

<sup>8</sup> Awake my tongue, awake nablum  <span style="color:#800000">ff85</span> and harp: I will awake at dawn of day.

<!-- PAGE 917 -->

<sup>9</sup> I will celebrate thee, among the peoples, O Lord! I will sing psalms to thee among the nation:

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>10</sup> For great unto the heavens is thy goodness, And unto the clouds thy truth.</p>

<sup>11</sup> Be thou exalted, above the heavens, O God! Above all the earth thy glory.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 58</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician. Destroy not. Michtam of David*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Do ye indeed, O ye congregation! speak righteousness? Uprightly do ye judge? O ye sons of men!

<sup>2</sup> Yea rather in *your* heart wickedness ye plot, On the earth violence your hands weigh out.

<sup>3</sup> Estranged are the ungodly from the womb, They have gone astray from their birth speaking falsehood.

<sup>4</sup> They have poison like the poison of a serpent, [*They are*] like the deaf adder which stoppeth her ear;

<sup>5</sup> Which listeneth not to the voice of the enchanter, — Of him who exerciseth enchantment skillfully.

<sup>6</sup> O God! break their teeth in their mouth: The jawbones of the lions break, O Jehovah!

<sup>7</sup> Let them melt away like water, let them be gone: Let them bend their bow, and let their arrows be as if broken.

<sup>8</sup> Like a snail which melteth away let them vanish; Like the untimely birth of a woman which doth not see the sun.

<sup>9</sup> Before your pots can feel the fire of the bramble, *Like* flesh yet raw as a whirlwind he shall carry him away.

<sup>10</sup> Rejoice shall the righteous when he seeth the vengeance; His hands he shall wash in the blood of the ungodly.

<!-- PAGE 918 -->

<sup>11</sup> And men  <span style="color:#800000">ff86</span> shall say, “Truly there is fruit for the righteous, “Truly there is a God who judgeth in the earth.”

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 59</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician. Destroy not. Michtam of David. When Saul sent, and*</span> <span style="color:#000080">*they watched the house to kill him*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Deliver me from my enemies, O my God! from those who rise up against me set me on high.

<sup>2</sup> Deliver me from the workers of iniquity, And from men of bloods save me:

<sup>3</sup> For lo! they have laid snares for my soul; Gathered together against me have the strong men; Not [*for*] my sin, nor [*for*] my wickedness, O Jehovah!

<sup>4</sup> Without *any* iniquity *of mine* they have run and prepared themselves. Awake and come to meet me, and behold.

<sup>5</sup> And thou, O Jehovah, God of armies! the God of Israel! Awake to visit all the nations; Do not have compassion upon any who transgress wickedly. Selah.

<sup>6</sup> They will return at evening; They will make a noise like a dog, And go round about the city.

<sup>7</sup> Behold they will prate with their mouth; Swords are in their lips; “For who,” *say they*, “will hear?”

<sup>8</sup> But thou, O Jehovah! shalt laugh at them; Thou shalt hold in derision all the nations.

<sup>9</sup> His strength with thee I will put in trust; For God is my fortress.

<sup>10</sup> The God of my mercy shall prevent me; God will make me see my desire upon my enemies.

<!-- PAGE 919 -->

<sup>11</sup> Slay them not, lest my people should forget; Make them to wander by thy power; And bring them down, O Lord, our shield!

<sup>12</sup> The sin of their mouth, the words of their lips: Let them be taken in their pride: of cursing and lying let them speak.

<sup>13</sup> Consume, in thy fury consume them, that they may be no more; And let men know that God ruleth in Jacob, Even to the ends of the earth. Selah. <span style="color:#800000">ff87</span> <sup>14</sup> And they will return at evening; They will bark like a dog, And go round about the city.

<sup>15</sup> They will wander up and down for food; If they be not satisfied they will even stay all night.

<sup>16</sup> But as for me, I will sing of thy strength, I will praise in the morning thy mercy; For thou hast been a fortress to me, And a refuge in the day of my trouble.

<sup>17</sup> My strength is with thee, I will sing psalms; For God *is* my fortress, the God of my mercy.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 60</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician upon Shushan Eduth. Michtam of David to teach.*</span> <span style="color:#000080">*When he fought against the Syrians of Mesopotamia and against the Syrians*</span> <span style="color:#000080">*of Zobah; and when Joab, having returned, smote of the Edomites in the*</span> <span style="color:#000080">*Valley of Salt twelve thousand*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> O God! thou hast cast us off, thou hast scattered us; Thou hast been angry, O return to us!

<sup>2</sup> To tremble thou hast made the earth; Thou hast caused it to open wide; Heal the breaches thereof for it shaketh.

<sup>3</sup> Thou hast showed to thy people a hard thing, Thou hast made us drunk with the wine of stupefaction.

<sup>4</sup> Thou hast given to those who fear thee a banner, That it may be displayed before *thy* truth. Selah.

<!-- PAGE 920 -->

<sup>5</sup> That thy beloved ones may be delivered, Save with thy right hand and hear me.

<sup>6</sup> God hath spoken in his holiness; I will rejoice: I will divide Shechem, And the valley of Succoth I will measure.

<sup>7</sup> Mine *shall be* Gilead, and mine *shall be* Manasseh; And Ephraim *shall be* the strength of my head; Judah, my lawgiver.

<sup>8</sup> Moab *shall be* the pot for my washing; Over Edom will I cast my shoe; O Palestina! triumph thou over me.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>9</sup> Who will bring me into the city fortified? Who will lead me into Edom?</p>

<sup>10</sup> Wilt not thou, O God! who hadst cast us off?  [*thou*] O God! [*who*] didst not go forth with our armies?

<sup>11</sup> Grant us relief from trouble, For vain *is* the help of man.

<sup>12</sup> Through God we shall do valiantly: And he it is who shall tread down our adversaries.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 61</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician upon Neginoth. A Psalm of David*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Hear, O God! my cry, Attend to my prayer.

<sup>2</sup> From the end of the earth to thee will I cry, when vexed is my heart: To the rock which is higher than I thou shalt lead me.

<sup>3</sup> For thou hast been a hope to me, A tower of strength from the face of the enemy.

<sup>4</sup> I will dwell in thy tabernacle for ever; I will be safe under the covert of thy wings. Selah.

<sup>5</sup> For thou, O God! hast heard my vows: Thou hast given an inheritance to those who fear thy name.

<!-- PAGE 921 -->

<sup>6</sup> Days upon days to the king thou shalt add, His years *shall be* as many generations.

<sup>7</sup> He shall dwell for ever in the presence of God: Mercy and truth do thou prepare: thou shalt preserve him.

<sup>8</sup> So will I sing unto thy name for ever, That I may perform my vows every day.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 62</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician upon Jeduthun. A Psalm of David*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Nevertheless towards God silent is my soul: From him is my salvation.

<sup>2</sup> Nevertheless he is my reek and my salvation, My fortress: *therefore* I shall not be moved greatly.

<sup>3</sup> How long will ye continue to lay snares against a man? Ye shall be slain all of you: As an inclining wall shall ye be, and a fence that is shaken.

<sup>4</sup> Yet from his elevation they consult to east him down:  They delight in falsehood: With their mouth they bless, And in their hearts they curse. Selah. Nevertheless towards God be thou silent O my soul: For from him *is* my expectation.

<sup>6</sup> Nevertheless he *only is* my rock, and my salvation: My fortress; I shall not fall.

<sup>7</sup> In God *is* my salvation and my glory; The rock of my strength; my hope *is* in God.

<sup>8</sup> Hope in him at all times, O ye people! Pour out before his face your hearts: God *is* our hope. Selah.

<!-- PAGE 922 -->
<sup>9</sup> Nevertheless vanity are the sons of Adam; A lie the sons of men: When put all together in a balance they are found lighter than vanity itself. <span style="color:#800000">ff88</span> <sup>10</sup> Trust not in oppression and robbery; be not vain: [*Upon*] riches if they abound set not your heart.

<sup>11</sup> Once God hath spoken; Twice this I have heard, “That power belongeth to God; “ And to thee, O Lord! *belongeth* mercy; Truly thou wilt render to every man according to his work.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 63</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*A Psalm of David, when he was in the wilderness of Judea*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> O God! my God art thou; Early will I seek thee: Thirsted for thee hath my soul, Longed for thee hath my flesh, In this land [*that is*] inhospitable and dry, without water.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>2</sup> Thus in the sanctuary have I beheld thee, To see thy power and thy glory.</p>

<sup>3</sup> Because better *is* thy mercy than life, My lips shall praise thee.

<sup>4</sup> Thus will I bless thee in my life; In thy name I will lift up my hands.

<sup>5</sup> As with marrow and farness satisfied shall be my soul, And with lips of rejoicing shall my mouth praise *thee*.

<sup>6</sup> Surely I will remember thee upon my bed; In the watches *of the night* I will meditate upon thee;

<sup>7</sup> Because thou hast been a help to me: And in the shadow of thy wings I will shout for joy.

<sup>8</sup> Cleaved hath my soul to [*literally after*] thee; Sustain me shall thy right hand.

<sup>9</sup> And they whilst they seek to destroy my soul, Shall go into the lowest parts of the earth.

<sup>10</sup> They shall east him down to the edge of the sword: The portion of foxes they shall be.

<!-- PAGE 923 -->

<sup>11</sup> But the king shall rejoice in God, And glory shall every one who sweareth by him: For stopped shall be the mouth of those who speak falsehood.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 64</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician. A Psalm of David*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Hear, O God! my voice in my prayer: From fear of the enemy preserve my life.

<sup>2</sup> Hide me from the counsel of malignant men — From the assembly of tire workers of iniquity.

<sup>3</sup> For they have sharpened as a sword their tongue; They have directed for their arrow a bitter word.

<sup>4</sup> To shoot in secret at the innocent man: Suddenly they will shoot and not fear.

<sup>5</sup> They encourage themselves in an impious action; They talk together of secretly laying snares; They say, “Who shall see them?”

<sup>6</sup> They have searched out iniquities, They have accomplished a searching search: And the inward part of each of them and the heart [*is*] deep.

<sup>7</sup> But God shall shoot at them an arrow; Suddenly shall they be wounded.

<sup>8</sup> And they shall make their own tongue fall upon themselves, And flee away shall all who see them.

<sup>9</sup> And see shall all men, and shall declare the work of God, And his doing they shall understand.

<sup>10</sup> Rejoice shall the righteous in Jehovah, and they shall hope in him; And glory shall all the upright in heart.

<!-- PAGE 924 -->

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 65</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician. A Psalm of David. A Song*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> For thee waiteth praise, O God! in Zion; And to thee performed shall be the vow.

<sup>2</sup> O thou who hearest prayer! To thee shall all flesh come.

<sup>3</sup> Words of iniquity  <span style="color:#800000">ff89</span> have prevailed against me: Our crimes thou shalt expiate.

<sup>4</sup> Blessed is he whom thou wilt choose, and cause to approach to thee; He shall dwell in thy courts: We shall be satisfied with the goodness of thy house — Of the sanctuary of thy palace.

<sup>5</sup> Terrible things in righteousness wilt thou answer us, O God of our salvation! The hope of all the ends of the earth, And of the far off places of the sea.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>6</sup> Establishing the mountains by his power,  <span style="color:#800000">ff90</span> Being girded with might.</p>

<sup>7</sup> Stilling the noise of the seas, And the noise of their waves, the tumults of the nations.

<sup>8</sup> And afraid shall be the dwellers in the ends *of the earth* at thy signs; The outgoings of the morning and evening thou shalt make to shout for joy.

<sup>9</sup> Thou hast visited the earth, and watered her; Thou hast abundantly enriched her: The river of God is full of waters: Thou wilt prepare their corn, For so thou hast prepared her.

<sup>10</sup> Her furrows thou dost saturate, Thou makest the rain to fall into her ridges, With showers thou dost moisten her, Her buddings thou dost bless.

<sup>11</sup> Thou crownest the year with thy beneficence, And thy paths will drop fatness.

<!-- PAGE 925 -->

<sup>12</sup> They drop upon the dwellings of the wilderness; And with gladness the hills shall be girded.

<sup>13</sup> Clothed are the pastures with flocks; And the valleys are covered with corn; They shout for joy, they also sing.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 66</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Justice,. A Song of a Psalm*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Shout joyfully to God all the earth, <sup>2</sup> Sing the glory of his name, Make glorious his praise.

<sup>3</sup> Say unto God: “How terrible art thou in thy works! “Through the greatness of thy power shall thy enemies feign submission unto thee.

<sup>4</sup> “All the earth shall worship thee, and shall sing to thee, “They shall hymn thy name.” Selah.

<sup>5</sup> Come ye, and see the works of God: He is terrible in his doing towards the sons of men.

<sup>6</sup> He turned the sea into dry *land*; Through the river  <span style="color:#800000">ff91</span> they passed on foot; There we were glad in him.

<sup>7</sup> He ruleth by his might over the world;  <span style="color:#800000">ff92</span> His eyes upon the nations are fixed: The rebels shall not exalt themselves. Selah.

<sup>8</sup> Bless, O ye people! our God, And resound the voice of his praise.

<sup>9</sup> Who hath brought our souls into life, And hath not suffered our feet to fall.

<sup>10</sup> For thou hast proved us, O God! Thou hast tried us as silver is tried:

<sup>11</sup> Thou hast brought us into the net Thou hast laid restraint upon our loins;

<!-- PAGE 926 -->

<sup>12</sup> Thou hast made man to ride over our heads; We have come into fire and water; And thou hast brought us into a place of abundance.

<sup>13</sup> I will come into thy house with burnt-offerings; I will pay thee my vows,

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>14</sup> Which my lips have uttered, And my mouth hath spoken in my affliction.</p>

<sup>15</sup> Burnt-offerings of fat *lambs* I will offer to thee with incense of rams; I will bring bullocks with goats. Selah.

<sup>16</sup> Come, hear, and I will tell all you who fear God, What he hath done for my soul.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>17</sup> To him with my mouth I cried, And I have exalted him with my tongue.</p>

<sup>18</sup> If I have regarded iniquity in my heart, The Lord will not hear me.

<sup>19</sup> But truly heard me hath God; He hath attended to the voice of my prayer.

<sup>20</sup> Blessed be God who hath not turned away my prayer, Nor his mercy from me.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 67</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician on Neginoth. A Psalm [*</span><span style="color:#000080">or</span><span style="color:#000080">*] Song*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> May God have compassion upon us, and bless us; May he cause his countenance to shine upon us. Selah.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>2</sup> That thy way may be known upon the earth, Among all nations thy salvation.</p>

<sup>3</sup> Let the peoples praise thee, O God! Let all the peoples celebrate thee.

<!-- PAGE 927 -->

<sup>4</sup> Let the peoples rejoice and exult, For he shall judge the peoples with rectitude, And the nations upon earth thou shalt guide. Selah.

<sup>5</sup> Let the peoples celebrate thee, O God! Let all the peoples celebrate thee:

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>6</sup> The earth hath given *her* increase; God [*even*] our own God will bless us.</p>

<sup>7</sup> God will bless us; And fear him shall all the ends of the earth.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 68</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician. A Psalm [ορ Song of David*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Arise shall God: scattered shall be his enemies; And flee from before his face shall those who hate him.

<sup>2</sup> As smoke is driven away thou shalt drive them away; As wax melteth before the fire, perish shall the ungodly from before the face of God.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>3</sup> But the righteous shall rejoice, They shell exult before God, And leap for joy.</p>

<sup>4</sup> Sing ye unto God, sing psalms to his name; Exalt him who rideth upon the clouds whose name is Jah, And exult before him.

<sup>5</sup> The father of the orphans, and the judge of the widows, *Is* God in the habitation of his holiness.

<sup>6</sup> God who causeth the solitary to dwell in a family, Who bringeth forth those who are bound with chains: But rebels shall dwell in a dry land.

<sup>7</sup> O God! when thou well test forth before thy people, When thou marchedst through the wilderness; Selah:

<!-- PAGE 928 -->

<sup>8</sup> The earth was moved, Also the heavens dropped at the presence of this God: Sinai at the presence of God, the God of Israel.

<sup>9</sup> A liberal rain thou shalt make to fall, O God! upon thy inheritance, And when it is weary thou refreshest it.

<sup>10</sup> Thy congregation shall dwell therein: Thou wilt provide in thy goodness for the poor, O God!

<sup>11</sup> The Lord shall give the word To the women who announce the great army.

<sup>12</sup> Kings of armies shall flee — shall flee; And she who dwelleth within the house shall divide the spoils.

<sup>13</sup> Though ye should lie among the pots, *in the ashes*, *Yet shall ye be as* the wings of the dove covered with silver, And *which* behind is *as* fine yellow gold.

<sup>14</sup> When the Almighty scattered kings in it, It was white in Salmon.

<sup>15</sup> The mountain of God, the mountain of Bashan, The mountain of heights, the mountain of Bashan.

<sup>16</sup> Why leap ye, ye mountains of heights? The mountain in which it hath pleased God to dwell: Yea dwell *in it* will Jehovah for ever.

<sup>17</sup> The chariots of God [*are*] twenty thousand thousands of angels: The Lord *is* among them, In the sanctuary as in Sinai.

<sup>18</sup> Thou hast ascended on high; Thou hast led captive captivity; Thou hast received gifts among men; Yea even [*among*] rebels, That Jehovah God might dwell *in the midst of his people*.

<sup>19</sup> Blessed *be* God day by day: *This* God will load us with deliverances. Selah.

<sup>20</sup> Our God *is* the God of salvations; And to Jehovah Lord *belong* the issues of death.

<!-- PAGE 929 -->

<sup>21</sup> Surely God shall wound the head of his enemies, The crown of the hair of him who walketh in his wickedness.

<sup>22</sup> The Lord hath said: “From Bashan I will bring back; “I will bring back from the depths of the sea:

<sup>23</sup> “That thy foot may be stained with blood, “The tongue of thy dogs even in that of thy enemies.”

<sup>24</sup> They have seen thy goings, O God!— The goings of my God, my King, in the sanctuary.

<sup>25</sup> Before went the singers; [*Then*] followed the players on instruments; In the midst [*were*] the damsels striking the timbrels.

<sup>26</sup> In the congregations bless ye God, [*Even*] the Lord, *ye who* are of the fountain of Israel!

<sup>27</sup> There *was* little Benjamin their ruler, The princes of Judah in their assembly, The princes of Zebulun, the princes of Naphthali.

<sup>28</sup> Commanded hath thy God thy strength; Strengthen, O God! what thou hast wrought in us.

<sup>29</sup> From thy temple upon Jerusalem To thee shall kings bring presents.

<sup>30</sup> Destroy the company of spearmen, The assembly of bulls, with the calves of the peoples, Treading with their feet upon pieces of silver: Scatter thou the peoples that in wars delight.

<sup>31</sup> Princes shall come out of Egypt; Ethiopia shall speedily stretch forth her hands to God.

<sup>32</sup> O ye kingdoms of the earth! sing unto God: Sing psalms to the Lord. Selah.

<!-- PAGE 930 -->
<sup>33</sup> To him who rideth upon the heavens of heavens *which are* of old: Lo! he shall send forth in his voice a voice of strength, <sup>34</sup> Ascribe ye strength to God over Israel; His majesty and his strength [*are*] in the clouds.

<sup>35</sup> Terrible art thou, O God! out of thy holy places: The God of Israel himself shall give strength and might to his people. Blessed *be* God!

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 69</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician upon Shoshannim. Of David*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Save me, O God! For entered have the waters even to my soul.

<sup>2</sup> I am sunk into deep mire where there is no standing place; I am come into the depths of waters, And the floods of water have overflowed me.

<sup>3</sup> I am weary with crying, Hoarse is my throat become therewith; Failed have my eyes with waiting for my God.

<sup>4</sup> More in number than the hairs of my head are those who hate me without cause: Increased are those who desire to destroy me, [*even*] my lying adversaries: That which I took not by violence, then I restored it.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>5</sup> O God! thou knowest my foolishness, And my faults from thee are not hidden.</p>

<sup>6</sup> Let not those be ashamed in me that wait for thee, O Lord, Jehovah of armies! Let not those be put to shame in me who seek thee, O God of Israel!

<sup>7</sup> For on thy account I have suffered reproach; Shame hath covered my face.

<sup>8</sup> A stranger I have been to my brethren, And an alien I am become to the children of my mother.

<sup>9</sup> For the zeal of thy house hath eaten me up; And the reproaches of those who reproached thee have fallen upon me.

<!-- PAGE 931 -->

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>10</sup> And I wept, my soul fasted; And that was for a reproach to me.</p>

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>11</sup> And I made my garment sackcloth; And I was to them for a jest.</p>

<sup>12</sup> Defame me do those who sit in the gate; And *I am* the songs of those who drink intoxicating liquor.

<sup>13</sup> But as for me my prayer [*is*] to thee, O Jehovah! *In the* time of *thy* favor, O God! In the multitude of thy mercy answer me, In the truth of thy salvation.

<sup>14</sup> Rescue me from the mire that I may not sink; That I may be delivered from my adversaries, And from the depths of waters.

<sup>15</sup> Let not the flood of waters overflow me; And let not the deep swallow me up; And let not the pit dose its mouth upon me.

<sup>16</sup> Answer me, O Jehovah! for good *is* thy mercy: In the multitude of thy compassions look upon me.

<sup>17</sup> And hide not thy face from thy servant, for I am afflicted: Hasten! answer me!

<sup>18</sup> Draw near to my soul, redeem it: On account of my adversaries deliver me.

<sup>19</sup> Thou knowest my reproach, and my confusion, and my ignominy: Before thee *are* all my adversaries.

<sup>20</sup> Reproach hath broken my heart, and I am afflicted; And I looked for some one to show compassion, but there was none; For comforters, but I found *them* not.

<sup>21</sup> And they put into my meat gall, And in my thirst they gave me vinegar to drink.

<sup>22</sup> Let their table be before them for a snare; And their prosperity for a net;

<sup>23</sup> Darkened let be their eyes, that they may not see; And cause their loins continually to tremble.

<!-- PAGE 932 -->

<sup>24</sup> Pour out upon them thy wrath; And let the fury of thy indignation take hold of them.

<sup>25</sup> Let their palace be desolate; In their tents let there not be a dweller.

<sup>26</sup> For him whom thou hast smitten they have persecuted; And to the grief of those whom thou hast wounded they have added.

<sup>27</sup> Add iniquity to their iniquity; And let them not enter into thy righteousness.

<sup>28</sup> Let them be blotted out of the book of the living; And with the righteous let them not be written.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>29</sup> But as for me I am poor and sorrowful: Thy salvation shall exalt me.</p>

<sup>30</sup> I will praise the name of God in a song, And I will magnify him with thanksgiving.

<sup>31</sup> And this will please Jehovah More than a young bullock that hath horns and hoofs.

<sup>32</sup> Seen it have the afflicted, And they shall rejoice at it who seek God; And live shall your heart.

<sup>33</sup> For hearkened hath Jehovah to the afflicted; And his prisoners he hath not despised.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>34</sup> Praise him let heaven and earth; The seas and whatever creepeth in them.</p>

<sup>35</sup> For God will save Zion, And will build the cities of Judah; And they shall dwell there, And possess it by inheritance.

<sup>36</sup> And the seed of his servants shall inherit it; And they who love his name shall dwell in it.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 70</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician. Of David. To call to remembrance*</span></p>

<!-- PAGE 933 -->

<sup>1</sup> O God! to deliver me, O Jehovah! to my aid, hasten.

<sup>2</sup> Let those be ashamed and confounded who seek after my soul; Let those be turned backward and put to confusion who desire my hurt.

<sup>3</sup> Let those be destroyed for a reward of their shame, Who say to me, “Aha! Aha!”

<sup>4</sup> Exult and rejoice in thee let all who seek thee; And let those say, “For ever may God be magnified” — Those who love thy salvation.

<sup>5</sup> But as for me I am poor and needy: O God! hasten to me: My help and my deliverer art thou: O Jehovah! delay not.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 71</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> In thee, O Jehovah! do I put my trust, Let me not be put to shame for ever.

<sup>2</sup> In thy righteousness deliver me, and rescue me; Incline to me thy ear, and save me.

<sup>3</sup> Be thou to me for a rock of strength, Into which I may enter at all times: Thou hast given commandment to save me; For my tower and my fortress art thou.

<sup>4</sup> O my God! deliver me from the hand of the ungodly man, — From the hand of the wicked and violent men.

<sup>5</sup> For thou art my expectation, O Lord Jehovah! My trust from my youth.

<sup>6</sup> Upon thee have I been sustained from the womb; Out of my mother’s bowels thou art he who took me: Of thee is my praise continually.

<sup>7</sup> As a prodigy I have been to the great ones, And yet thou art my strong confidence.

<!-- PAGE 934 -->

<sup>8</sup> Filled shall be my mouth with thy praise, Daily with thy glory.

<sup>9</sup> Cast me not off in the time of my old age: In the declining of my strength forsake me not.

<sup>10</sup> For my enemies have spoken concerning me, And those who watch for my soul have consulted together, <sup>11</sup> Saying, “God hath forsaken him; “Follow after him, and ye shall take him; “For there is none to deliver *him*.”

<sup>12</sup> O God! be not far from me: O my God! to my aid hasten.

<sup>13</sup> Let those be put to shame, let those fail who are the adversaries of my soul: Let those be covered with reproach mid shame who seek my hurt.

<sup>14</sup> But as for me continually will I hope, And will add to all thy praise.

<sup>15</sup> My mouth shall tell of thy righteousness, Daily of thy salvation; For I know not the number *thereof*.

<sup>16</sup> I will go in the strength of the Lord Jehovah! I will remember thy righteousness alone.

<sup>17</sup> O God! thou hast taught me from my youth; And unto this time will I announce thy wonders.

<sup>18</sup> And therefore  <span style="color:#800000">ff93</span> in old age and gray hairs, O God! forsake me not, Until I have declared thy strength to the generation, To all who are to come thy might.

<sup>19</sup> And thy righteousness, O God! is very high: For thou hast done great things: O God! who is like to thee?

<sup>20</sup> Thou hast made me to see troubles many and grievous, *But* being turned thou wilt quicken me, And from the depths of the earth, being turned thou wilt raise me up.

<sup>21</sup> Thou wilt multiply my greatness; And being turned thou wilt comfort me.

<!-- PAGE 935 -->

<sup>22</sup> Moreover, I, even I, will sing to thee on instruments of music; <span style="color:#800000">ff94</span> For thy truth, O my God! I will sing psalms to thee upon the harp, O Holy One of Israel!

<sup>23</sup> Exult shall my lips, when I sing psalms to thee; And my soul which thou hast redeemed.

<sup>24</sup> Also my tongue daily shall declare thy righteousness; For they are put to shame, for they are disgraced, who seek my hurt.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 72</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*Of Solomon*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> O God! thy judgments give to the King, And thy righteousness to the son of the King <sup>2</sup> He shall judge thy people in righteousness, And thy poor ones in judgment.

<sup>3</sup> Bring forth shall the mountains peace to the people, And the hills in righteousness.

<sup>4</sup> He shall judge the poor ones of the people; He shall save the children of the afflicted; And shall break in pieces the calumniator.

<sup>5</sup> They shall fear thee with the sun; And before the moon the generation of generations shall fear thee.

<sup>6</sup> He shall descend as rain upon the mown grass; As the showers which water the earth.

<sup>7</sup> Flourish in his days shall the righteous; And abundance of peace until there be no more moon.

<sup>8</sup> And he shall have dominion from sea to sea, And from the river even to the ends of the earth.

<sup>9</sup> Before him shall bow the inhabitants of the desert, And his enemies shall lick the dust.

<!-- PAGE 936 -->

<sup>10</sup> The kings of Tarshish and of the isles a present shall bring: The kings of Sheba and Seba a gift shall offer *to him*.

<sup>11</sup> And all kings shall prostrate themselves before him; All nations shall serve him.

<sup>12</sup> For he shall deliver the poor who crieth *to him*, And the needy who hath no helper.

<sup>13</sup> He will have compassion upon the indigent and the poor one, And the souls of the poor ones he will save.

<sup>14</sup> From fraud and violence he will redeem their souls, And precious shall be their blood in his eyes.

<sup>15</sup> And he shall live, and there shall be given to him of the gold of Sheba, And prayer shall be made for him continually; Daily shall he be blessed.

<sup>16</sup> There shall be *sown* an handful of corn in the earth on the top of the mountains; The fruit thereof shall be shaken like [*that*] of Lebanon. And they shall go forth from the city as a plant of the earth.

<sup>17</sup> His name shall be for ever; In presence of the sun propagated shall be his name: And bless themselves in him shall all nations, And blessed they shall call him.

<sup>18</sup> Blessed be Jehovah God! the God of Israel! <span style="color:#800000">ff95</span> Who doeth wonderful things alone.

<sup>19</sup> And blessed be his glorious name for ever; And let all the earth be filled with his glory. Amen and Amen.

<sup>20</sup> Finished are the prayers of David, the son of Jesse.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1">PART THIRD<sup>96</sup></p>

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 73</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*A Psalm of Asaph*</span></p>

<!-- PAGE 937 -->

<sup>1</sup> Yet God is good to Israel, To those who are right in heart.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>2</sup> As for me, wellnigh slipped had my feet, My steps were almost gone:</p>

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>3</sup> For I was envious at the foolish, On seeing the prosperity of the ungodly.</p>

<sup>4</sup> For there are no bands to their death, And vigorous is their strength.

<sup>5</sup> In the [*common*] trouble of man they are not; And with other men they are not scourged.

<sup>6</sup> Therefore encompass them as a chain doth pride; Covered them hath the raiment of violence.

<sup>7</sup> Their eye projecteth through fatness; They have passed beyond the thoughts of their heart.

<sup>8</sup> They break forth, and talk wickedly of extortion: From on high do they speak.

<sup>9</sup> They have set against the heaven their mouth, And their tongue walketh through the earth.

<sup>10</sup> On this account his people will return hither, And waters of a full cup will be wrung out to them.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>11</sup> And they say, “How doth God know? “And is there knowledge in the Most High? “</p>

<sup>12</sup> Behold these are the ungodly, and [*yet*]. they are in quiet for ever: They heap up riches.

<sup>13</sup> Surely in vain I have purified my heart, And washed every day my hands:

<sup>14</sup> And I have been scourged every day, And my chastisement [*has been*] every morning.

<sup>15</sup> If I have said, “I will speak thus,” Behold! the generation of thy children: I have transgressed.

<!-- PAGE 938 -->

<sup>16</sup> And although I applied my mind to know this, A pain fill thing it was in mine eyes, <sup>17</sup> Until I entered into the sanctuaries of God, *And* understood their end.
