<sup>18</sup> Surely in slippery places thou hast set them; Thou shalt east them down into destructions.

<sup>19</sup> How have they been destroyed as in a moment! They have perished, they have been consumed with terrors.

<sup>20</sup> As it were a dream after a man is awakened; O Lord! in awaking, their image thou wilt render contemptible.

<sup>21</sup> For in a ferment was my heart, And in my reins I was pierced.

<sup>22</sup> And I myself [*was*] foolish and knew not *any thing*; As the beasts have I been before thee.

<sup>23</sup> Nevertheless *I have been* continually with thee; Thou hast held my right hand.

<sup>24</sup> With thy counsel thou shalt guide me; And at length to glory thou shalt take me.

<sup>25</sup> Whom have I in heaven *but thee*? And another with time I have not desired on the earth.

<sup>26</sup> Failed have my flesh and my heart; *But* the strength of my heart and my portion *is* God for ever.

<sup>27</sup> For lo! those who depart from thee shall perish: Thou hast destroyed every one who goeth a whoring from thee.

<sup>28</sup> As for me, to draw near to God is good for me: I have put in the Lord Jehovah my trust, That I may tell of all thy works.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 74</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*An Instruction of Asaph*</span></p>

<!-- PAGE 939 -->

<sup>1</sup> Wherefore, O God! hast thou cast us off for ever? [*Why*] smoketh thy wrath against the flock of thy pastures?

<sup>2</sup> Remember thy congregation which thou hast possessed of old; <span style="color:#800000">ff97</span> *Which* thou hast redeemed, the rod of thy inheritance, This Mount Zion in which thou hast dwelt.

<sup>3</sup> Lift up thy strokes to desolations perpetual, Against every enemy who doeth mischief to the sanctuary.

<sup>4</sup> Roared *like lions* have thy adversaries in the midst of thy sanctuaries; They have set up their signs *for* signs.

<sup>5</sup> He was renowned as doing an excellent work Who lifted up the axes upon the thick trees. <span style="color:#800000">ff98</span> <sup>6</sup> And now the carved works thereof together, With the hatchet and with hammers they break in pieces.

<sup>7</sup> They have set on fire thy sanctuaries; Leveling it with the ground, they have polluted the dwelling-place of thy name.

<sup>8</sup> They have said in their heart, “Let us destroy them altogether.” They have burned all the tabernacles of God in the land.

<sup>9</sup> Our signs we see not; There is no longer a prophet, Nor is there with us one who knoweth how long! — <sup>10</sup> How long, O God! shall the adversary reproach? Shall the enemy blaspheme thy name for ever?

<sup>11</sup> How long wilt thou withdraw thy hand, and thy right hand? In the midst, of thy bosom consume them.

<sup>12</sup> But God *is* my king from the beginning, Working salvations in the midst of the earth.

<sup>13</sup> Thou didst divide  <span style="color:#800000">ff99</span> by thy power the sea; Thou didst break the heads of dragons in the waters.

<sup>14</sup> Thou didst break the head of the leviathan; Thou gavest him *for* food to *thy* people in the wilderness.

<!-- PAGE 940 -->

<sup>15</sup> Thou didst cleave the fountain and the torrent: Thou didst dry up mighty rivers.

<sup>16</sup> Thine is the day; thine also is the night: Thou hast ordained the light and the sun.

<sup>17</sup> Thou hast fixed all the boundaries of the earth: The summer and the winter thou hast made.

<sup>18</sup> Remember this: the enemy hath reproached Jehovah; And a worthless people hath done despite to thy name.

<sup>19</sup> Give not to the beast the soul of thy turtle dove: The congregation of thy poor ones forget not for ever.

<sup>20</sup> Have regard to *thy* covenant; For filled are the dark places of the earth with the abodes of violence.

<sup>21</sup> Let not the afflicted one return ashamed; Let the poor and needy one praise thy name.

<sup>22</sup> Arise, O God! plead thy own cause: Remember thy reproach, *which is done thee* by the foolish man daily.

<sup>23</sup> Forget not the voice of thy adversaries: The tumult of those who rise up against thee ascendeth continually.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 75</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician. Destroy not. A Psalm of Asaph. A song*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> We will celebrate thee, O God! we will celebrate *thee*; And near is thy name: they will declare thy wonders.

<sup>2</sup> When I shall have taken the congregation, I in uprightness will judge.

<sup>3</sup> Dissolved is the earth, and all the inhabitants thereof: will establish the pillars of it. Selah.

<sup>4</sup> I said to the fools, “Act not foolishly; “ And to the ungodly, “Raise not up the horn.”

<!-- PAGE 941 -->

<sup>5</sup> Raise not up on high your horn; And speak not with a stiff neck. g. For neither from the east, nor from the west, Nor from the wilderness are exaltations.

<sup>7</sup> For God is judge: tie bringeth low, and he setteth up.

<sup>8</sup> For a cup *is* in the hand of Jehovah, and the wine is turbid; It is full of mixture, and he shall pour forth of it. Surely the dregs thereof they shall wring out;. And drink *of it* shall all the wicked of the earth.

<sup>9</sup> But as for me I will publish for ever, I will sing psalms to the God of Jacob.

<sup>10</sup> And all the horns of the ungodly I will break; But exalted shall be the horns of the righteous.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 76</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician on Neginoth. A Psalm of Asaph. A Song*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Known in Judah is God; In Israel great is his name.

<sup>2</sup> And in Salem was his tabernacle, And his dwelling-place in Zion.

<sup>3</sup> There he broke the arrows of the bow, The shield, and the sword, and the battle. Selah.

<sup>4</sup> More glorious, more terrible *art* thou Than the mountains of prey.

<sup>5</sup> Spoiled were the stout in heart, They slept their sleep: And none of the men of might have found their hands.

<sup>6</sup> At thy rebuke, O God of Jacob! Cast into a deep sleep were the chariot and the horse.

<sup>7</sup> Thou *art* terrible [*even*] thou; And who shall stand before thy face when thou art angry?

<!-- PAGE 942 -->

<sup>8</sup> From the heavens thou hast made judgment to be heard: The earth was afraid and was still,

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>9</sup> When God arose to judgment, To save all the meek of the earth. Selah.</p>

<sup>10</sup> Surely the wrath of men shall praise thee, The remainder of wrath thou wilt restrain.

<sup>11</sup> Vow and pay to Jehovah your God: Let all who are round about him bring a present to the terrible one.

<sup>12</sup> He will cut off the spirit of princes: *He is* terrible to the kings of the earth.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 77</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician upon Jeduthun. A Psalm of Asaph*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> My voice [*I lifted up*] to God, and I cried; My voice [*I lifted up*] to God, and he heard me.

<sup>2</sup> In the day of my affliction the Lord I sought: My hand in the night was stretched out and relaxed not; Refuse consolation did my soul.

<sup>3</sup> I will remember God, and will be disquieted; I will meditate, and oppressed with sorrow shall be my spirit. Selah.

<sup>4</sup> Thou hast held the watches of my eyes: I am troubled, and will not speak.

<sup>5</sup> I have recounted the days of old, The years of ages past.

<sup>6</sup> I will call to remembrance my song in the night: With my heart I will meditate, And search diligently shall my spirit.

<sup>7</sup> For ever will the Lord east off? And will he be favorable no more?

<sup>8</sup> Gone for ever is his mercy? Fail doth his oracle from generation to generation?

<!-- PAGE 943 -->

<sup>9</sup> Forgotten to be merciful hath God? Hath he shut up in anger his compassions? Selah.

<sup>10</sup> And I said my disease [*is only for a season*;] The years of the right hand of the Most High [*I will wait for*.] <sup>11</sup> I will remember the works of God; Surely I will remember thy wonders from the beginning.

<sup>12</sup> And I will meditate on all thy works, And on thy doings I will muse.

<sup>13</sup> O God! in the sanctuary *are* thy ways: What god *is* so great as God?

<sup>14</sup> Thou art the God who doest wonders; Thou hast made known among the peoples thy strength.

<sup>15</sup> Thou hast redeemed by [*thy*] arm thy people, The sons of Jacob and Joseph. Selah.

<sup>16</sup> See thee did the waters, O God! see thee did the waters; They were afraid; also tremble did the deeps.

<sup>17</sup> The clouds poured out waters; The heavens gave forth a voice: Also thine arrows went abroad.

<sup>18</sup> The voice of thy thunder was in the circuit, The lightnings illumined the world; The earth trembled and was shaken.

<sup>19</sup> In the sea are thy ways, And thy paths in the great waters; And thy footsteps are not known.

<sup>20</sup> Thou leadest like sheep thy people By the hand of Moses and Aaron.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 78</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*A lesson of Instruction [οφ Asaph*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Give ear, O my people! to my law: Incline your ears to the words of my month.

<!-- PAGE 944 -->

<sup>2</sup> I will open in a parable my mouth; I will utter enigmas from ancient Sae.

<sup>3</sup> What we have heard and known, And our’ fathers have related to us, <sup>4</sup> We will not conceal from their children to the generation to come, Recounting the praises of Jehovah and his might, And the wonders which he hath done.

<sup>5</sup> And he established a testimony in, Jacob, And a law he appointed in Israel; For lie commanded our fathers To make them known to their children;

<sup>6</sup> That the generations to come might know them, *That* the children to be born might arise, And declare *them* to their children.

<sup>7</sup> That they might set in God their hope, And not forget the works of God; But that his commandments they might observe, <sup>8</sup> And not be as their fathers, A generation rebellious and provoking; A generation which set not their heart might, And whose spirit was not faithful towards God.

<sup>9</sup> The children of Ephraim, armed [*and*] shooting with the bow, Were turned back in the day of battle.

<sup>10</sup> They kept not the covenant of God, And in his law they refused to walk.

<sup>11</sup> And they forget his works, And the wonders which he had shown them.

<sup>12</sup> In the sight of their fathers he wrought marvelously; In the land of Egypt, in the field of Zoan.

<sup>13</sup> He divided the sea, and caused them to pass through, And made the waters to stand as an heap.

<sup>14</sup> And he led them by a cloud in the day; And all the night by the light of fire.

<!-- PAGE 945 -->

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>15</sup> He clave the rocks in the wilderness, And made them to drink in great deeps.</p>

<sup>16</sup> And he brought forth streams from the rock, And made the waters to descend like rivers.

<sup>17</sup> Yet they continued still to sin against him, To provoke the Most High in the wilderness.

<sup>18</sup> And they tempted God in their heart, By asking food for their soul.

<sup>19</sup> And they spake against God: They said, “Can God prepare a table in the wilderness?”

<sup>20</sup> Behold! he smote the reek, and gush out did the waters; And rivers overflowed. “Can he give bread also? “Will he prepare flesh for his people?”

<sup>21</sup> Therefore Jehovah heard and was wroth: And a fire was kindled in Jacob: And wrath also ascended against Israel;

<sup>22</sup> Because they believed not in God, And trusted not in his salvation.

<sup>23</sup> But he had commanded the clouds from above, And the doors of heaven he had opened, <sup>24</sup> And had rained down upon them manna for food, And the corn of heaven he had given them:

<sup>25</sup> The bread of the mighty ones man had eaten: Meat he had sent them to the full.

<sup>26</sup> He caused to blow an east wind in the heavens; And he raised up by his power the south wind.

<sup>27</sup> And he rained upon them flesh as dust, And as the sand of the sea leathered fowl;

<sup>28</sup> And he caused *it* to fall in the midst of his camp, Round about his tabernacles.

<!-- PAGE 946 -->

<sup>29</sup> And they did eat and were filled exceedingly, And their desire he brought to them.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>30</sup> They were not estranged from their desire: The meat was still in their mouth,</p>

<sup>31</sup> When the wrath of God ascended against them, And slew their fat ones, And brought low the chosen of Israel.

<sup>32</sup> In all these *things* they sinned still, And believed not his wonders.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>33</sup> And he consumed in vanity their days, And their years in haste.</p>

<sup>34</sup> When he slew them, they sought him; They returned, and hastened early to God.

<sup>35</sup> And they remembered that God was their Rock, And *that* the Most High God was their Redeemer.

<sup>36</sup> And they flattered [*him*] with their mouth, And with their tongue they lied to him:

<sup>37</sup> But their heart was not right before him, And they were not faithful in his covenant.

<sup>38</sup> Yet he *who is* compassionate expiated their iniquity, And did not destroy them: And he multiplied to turn away his anger, And did not stir up all his indignation.

<sup>39</sup> And he remembered that they were flesh; A breath that passeth, and returneth not.

<sup>40</sup> How often did they provoke him in the desert, [*And*] grieve him in the wilderness!

<sup>41</sup> And they returned and tempted God, And the Holy One of Israel they limited.

<!-- PAGE 947 -->
<sup>42</sup> They remembered not his hand In the day that he redeemed them from the oppressor, <sup>43</sup> When he set in Egypt his signs, And his miracles in the field of Zoan.

<sup>44</sup> When he turned into blood their rivers; And their streams, that they might not drink of them.

<sup>45</sup> He sent among them a mixture which devoured them; And the frog which destroyed them.

<sup>46</sup> And he gave to the caterpillar their fruit; And their labors to the locusts. <span style="color:#800000">ff100</span> <sup>47</sup> And he destroyed with hail their vine, And their wild fig-trees with hailstones.

<sup>48</sup> And he gave lip to the hail their cattle, And their flocks to thunderbolts.

<sup>49</sup> He sent upon them the fierceness of his wrath, Fury, anger, and affliction, A mission of evil angels.

<sup>50</sup> He made a path for his anger: He kept not from death their soul, And their cattle to the pestilence he shut up.

<sup>51</sup> And he smote all the firstborn in Egypt: The beginning of [*their*] strength in the tents of Ham.

<sup>52</sup> And he made to go forth like sheep his people, And led them like a flock in the wilderness.

<sup>53</sup> And he conducted *them* in safety, and they were not afraid: And their enemies cover did the sea.

<sup>54</sup> And he brought them to the border of his holiness, This mountain, *which* his right hand acquired.

<sup>55</sup> And he expelled from before their face the heathen; And made them to fall into the lot of all inheritance; And caused to dwell in their tents the children of Israel.

<sup>56</sup> But they tempted and provoked God Most High, And his testimonies they kept not.

<!-- PAGE 948 -->

<sup>57</sup> And they turned back and dealt perfidiously like their fathers: They started aside like a bow that is deceitful.

<sup>58</sup> And they provoked him to anger with their high places; And with their graven images they moved him to anger.

<sup>59</sup> Hear it did God, and was wroth, And he abhorred exceedingly Israel.

<sup>60</sup> And he forsook the habitation of Shiloh, The tabernacle where he dwelt among men.

<sup>61</sup> And he gave into captivity his strength, And his beauty into the hand of the enemy.

<sup>62</sup> And he shut up to the sword his people, And with his own inheritance was wroth.

<sup>63</sup> Their chosen devour did the fire, And their virgins were not applauded.

<sup>64</sup> Their priests by the sword did fall; And their widows made no lamentation.

<sup>65</sup> But the Lord awoke as one asleep, As a mighty man who crieth out by reason of wine.

<sup>66</sup> And he smote his enemies behind; Everlasting disgrace he put upon them.

<sup>67</sup> And he rejected the tabernacle of Joseph, And the tribe of Ephraim he chose not:

<sup>68</sup> But he chose the tribe of Judah, The mountain of Zion, which lie loved:

<sup>69</sup> And built like high places his sanctuary, Like the earth which he hath founded for ever.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>70</sup> And he chose David his servant, And took him from the folds of sheep:</p>

<!-- PAGE 949 -->

<sup>71</sup> From following the suckling ewes he took him, To feed Jacob his people, And Israel his inheritance:

<sup>72</sup> And he fed them in the integrity of his heart, And by the prudence of his hands he guided there.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 79</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*A Psalm of Asaph*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> O God! come have the heathen into thy inheritance; They have polluted the temple of thy holiness; They have laid Jerusalem in heaps. [fowls of the heavens;

<sup>2</sup> They have given the dead bodies of thy servants for food to the fowls of the heavens; The flesh of thy meek ones to the beasts of the earth.

<sup>3</sup> They have shed their blood like water around Jerusalem: And there was not *one* to bury *them*.

<sup>4</sup> We have been a reproach to our neighbors; A scorn and a derision to those who are around us.

<sup>5</sup> How long, O Jehovah! wilt thou be wroth for ever? Burn like fire shall thy jealousy?

<sup>6</sup> Pour out thy wrath upon the heathen who have not known thee, And upon the kingdoms which upon thy name do not call:

<sup>7</sup> For they have devoured Jacob, And his dwelling they have laid waste.

<sup>8</sup> Remember not against us former iniquities: Make haste, let thy compassions prevent us; For we are afflicted exceedingly.

<sup>9</sup> Help us, O God of our salvation! for the glory of thy name; And deliver us, and be merciful to our sins, for the sake of thy name.

<!-- PAGE 950 -->

<sup>10</sup> Wherefore should the heathen say, “Where is their God?” Let be made known among the heathen in our sight The vengeance of the blood of thy servants which is shed.

<sup>11</sup> Let the sighing of the prisoner come before thy face: According to the greatness of thy arm, Reserve the children of death:  <span style="color:#800000">ff101</span> <sup>12</sup> And recompense our neighbors sevenfold into their bosom, Their reproach with which they have reproached thee, O Jehovah!

<sup>13</sup> And we thy people, and the sheep of thy pastures, Will confess to thee for ever; From generation to generation declaring thy praise.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 80</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician upon Shoshannim Eduth. A Psalm of Asaph*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> O Shepherd of Israel, give ear! Who leadest Joseph like a flock; Thou who sittest between the cherubim, shine forth.

<sup>2</sup> Before Ephraim, and Benjamin, and Manasseh, Stir up thy strength, and come for our salvation.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>3</sup> Turn us again, O God! and cause to shine thy face, And we shall be safe.</p>

<sup>4</sup> O Jehovah God of armies! How long wilt thou be incensed against the prayer of thy people?

<sup>5</sup> Thou hast fed us with bread of tears; And hast given us tears to drink in great measure.

<sup>6</sup> Thou hast made us a strife to our neighbors: And our enemies laugh *at us* among themselves.

<sup>7</sup> O God of armies! turn us again, And cause to shine thy face upon us, And we shall be safe.

<!-- PAGE 951 -->

<sup>8</sup> A vine out of Egypt thou hast brought: Thou hast expelled the heathen, and planted it,.

<sup>9</sup> Thou hast cleansed *the ground* before it: Thou hast rooted its roots, And it hath filled the land.

<sup>10</sup> Covered were the mountains with its shadow, And its branches *were like* the cedars of God.

<sup>11</sup> It sent forth its branches to the sea, And to the river its shoots.

<sup>12</sup> Why then hast thou broken down its hedges, So that tear it in pieces do all who pass by the way?

<sup>13</sup> Wasted it hath the boar out of the forest; And the wild beast of the field hath eaten it up.

<sup>14</sup> O God of armies! return, I beseech *thee*, Look down from heaven, and behold, And visit this vine, <sup>15</sup> And the vineyard which planted hath thy right hand, And [*look*] upon the shoot  <span style="color:#800000">ff102</span> *which* thou hast strengthened for thyself.

<sup>16</sup> It is burnt with fire; it is cut down; At the rebuke of thy countenance they perish.

<sup>17</sup> Let thy hand be upon the Man of thy right hand, Upon the son of man whom thou hast strengthened for thyself, <sup>18</sup> And we will not go back from thee: Thou shalt quicken us, and upon thy name we will call.

<sup>19</sup> O Jehovah, God of armies! turn us again; Cause to shine thy face, And we shall be safe.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 81</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician upon Gittith. Of Asaph*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Exult to God our strength: Shout for joy to the God of Jacob.

<!-- PAGE 952 -->

<sup>2</sup> Take a song, and bring forth the timbrel, The harp of pleasantness with the nablum.  <span style="color:#800000">ff103</span> <sup>3</sup> Sound the trumpet  <span style="color:#800000">ff104</span> at the new moon; At the time appointed on the day of our sacrifice.

<sup>4</sup> For a statute to Israel *is* this, A judgment to the God of Jacob.

<sup>5</sup> For a testimony in Joseph he set it, When he went forth over the land of Egypt! A language which I understood not I heard.

<sup>6</sup> “I removed from the burden his shoulder: “His hands from the pots were removed.

<sup>7</sup> “In trouble thou didst cry, and I rescued thee: “I answered thee in the secret place of thunder: “I proved thee at the waters of Meribah. Selah.

<sup>8</sup> “Hear, O my people! and I will protest to thee: “O Israel! if thou wilt hearken to me.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>9</sup> “Let there not be in thee a strange god: “Neither worship thou a foreign god.</p>

<sup>10</sup> “I *am* Jehovah thy God, “Who brought thee out of the land of Egypt: “Open wide thy mouth, and I will fill it.

<sup>11</sup> “But my people hearkened not to my voice, “And Israel would have none of me.

<sup>12</sup> “And I gave them up to the hardness of their heart: “They shall walk in their own counsels.

<sup>13</sup> “O if my people had hearkened to me! “*If* Israel in my ways had walked!

<sup>14</sup> “Soon their enemies I would have brought low, “And against their adversaries I would have turned my hand:

<sup>15</sup> “The haters of Jehovah would have lied to him, “And their time should have been for ever:

<!-- PAGE 953 -->

<sup>16</sup> “And I would have fed them with the fat of corn: “And from the rock with honey I would have satisfied thee.”

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 82</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*A Psalm of Asaph*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> God sitteth in the assembly of God: In the midst of the gods he will judge.

<sup>2</sup> How long will ye judge unjustly? And the faces of the ungodly will ye lift up? Selah.

<sup>3</sup> Judge the poor and the orphan; The helpless and the destitute justify ye.

<sup>4</sup> Rescue the poor one and the afflicted one: From the hand of the ungodly ones deliver *them*.

<sup>5</sup> *But* they know not, neither do they understand *any thing*:  In darkness they walk, *Although* moved are all the foundations of the earth.

<sup>6</sup> As for me, I have said, “Ye are gods, “And children of the Most High all of you:

<sup>7</sup> “Surely as a man ye shall die; “And as one [*of the people*,] O princes! ye shall fall.”

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>8</sup> Arise, O God! judge the earth: For thou shalt inherit all nations.</p>

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 83</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*A Song [ορ Psalm, of Asaph*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> O God! keep not silence with thyself; Hold not thy peace, and be not still, O God!

<sup>2</sup> For behold! thy enemies make a tumult: And those who hate thee have lifted up the head.

<!-- PAGE 954 -->

<sup>3</sup> Against thy people they have craftily formed a design, And have consulted against thy hidden ones.

<sup>4</sup> They have said, “Come and let us cut them off from being a nation; “And let there be no remembrance of the name of Israel any more.”

<sup>5</sup> For they have consulted with the heart together; Against thee a covenant they have made.

<sup>6</sup> The tents of Edom, and of the Ishmaelites; Of Moab and of the Hagarenes.

<sup>7</sup> Gebal, and Ammon, and Amalek; The Philistines with the inhabitants of Tyre.

<sup>8</sup> Also Assur is associated with them: They have been an arm to the sons of Lot. Selah.

<sup>9</sup> Do to them as to the Midianites, As to Sisera, as to Jabin, at the brook Kishon.

<sup>10</sup> They perished at Endor; They became manure for the earth.

<sup>11</sup> Make them, [*even*] their princes, like Oreb, and like Zeeb; And like Zebah, and like Zalmunna, all their princes.

<sup>12</sup> Who have said, “Let us take in possession for ourselves “The habitations of God.”

<sup>13</sup> O my God! make them like a whirling ball; Like stubble before the wind.

<sup>14</sup> As fire burneth a forest, And as the flame kindleth the mountains, <sup>15</sup> So pursue them with thy tempest, And with thy whirlwind terrify them.

<sup>16</sup> Fill their faces with shame; That they may seek thy name, O Jehovah!

<!-- PAGE 955 -->

<sup>17</sup> Let them be ashamed, and terrified for ever;  <span style="color:#800000">ff105</span> And let them be confounded, and perish.

<sup>18</sup> And let them know that thou, even thou, art, thy name Jehovah, Thou alone the Most High over all the earth.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 84</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To The Chief Musician Upon Gittith. A Psalm of the sons of Korah*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> How amiable are thy tabernacles, O Jehovah of armies!

<sup>2</sup> Long, yea, even faint doth my soul after the courts of Jehovah: My heart and my flesh leap for joy towards the living God.

<sup>3</sup> Also the sparrow hath found a house for herself, And the swallow a nest for herself, Where she may place her young ones: O thine altars! thou Jehovah of armies! my King and my God!

<sup>4</sup> Blessed *are* those who dwell in thy house: Continually they will be praising thee. Selah.

<sup>5</sup> Blessed *is* the man whose strength is in thee; The ways [*are*] in their heart.

<sup>6</sup> They passing through the valley of weeping, will together make it a fountain; Also the cisterns the rain will cover.

<sup>7</sup> They will journey from strength to strength; Seen will be the God of gods in Zion.

<sup>8</sup> O Jehovah, God of armies! hear my prayer: Hearken, O God of Jacob! Selah.

<sup>9</sup> Our shield do thou behold, O God! And look upon the face of thy Christ.

<sup>10</sup> For better is one day in thy courts than a thousand elsewhere. I would rather be a doorkeeper in the house of my God, Than dwell in the tents of ungodliness.

<!-- PAGE 956 -->

<sup>11</sup> For a sun and shield *is* Jehovah God *to us*; Grace and glory give will Jehovah; He will not withhold *any* good from those who walk uprightly.

<sup>12</sup> O Jehovah of armies! Blessed *is* the man who confideth in thee.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 85</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*To the Chief Musician. A Psalm of the sons of Korah*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Thou hast been favorable, O Jehovah! to thy land: Thou hast brought back the captivity of Jacob.

<sup>2</sup> Thou hast taken away the iniquity of thy people: Thou hast covered all their sins. Selah <sup>3</sup> Thou hast turned away all thy anger: Thou hast drawn back the fury of thy indignation.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>4</sup> Turn us, O God of our salvation! And relax thy anger against us.</p>

<sup>5</sup> Wilt thou for ever be angry with us? Wilt thou prolong thy wrath from age to age?

<sup>6</sup> Wilt not thou being turned *to us* quicken us? And thy people will rejoice in thee.

<sup>7</sup> Show us, O Jehovah! thy mercy; and thy salvation grant unto us.

<sup>8</sup> I will hear what God Jehovah will speak: Surely he will speak peace to his people and to his meek ones, And they will not return to folly.

<sup>9</sup> Surely near to those who fear him is his salvation, That dwell may glory in our land.

<sup>10</sup> Mercy and truth shall meet together; Righteousness and peace shall kiss [*each other*.] <sup>11</sup> Truth out of the earth shall spring up; And righteousness from heaven shall look down.

<!-- PAGE 957 -->

<sup>12</sup> Likewise Jehovah will give that which is good: And our land shall yield her increase.

<sup>13</sup> Righteousness before him shall go; And shall set in the way her steps.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 86</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*A Prayer of David*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Incline, O Jehovah, thy ear, answer me; For poor and needy *am* I.

<sup>2</sup> Keep my soul, for I am meek: O thou, my God! save thy servant who trusteth in thee.

<sup>3</sup> Have compassion upon me, O Jehovah! For to thee do I cry daily.

<sup>4</sup> Gladden the soul of thy servant; For to thee, O Lord! my soul I lift up.

<sup>5</sup> For thou, O Lord! *art* good, and gracious, Plenteous in mercy to all who call upon thee.

<sup>6</sup> Listen, O Jehovah! to my prayer, Attend to the voice of my supplications.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>7</sup> In the day of my trouble I will cry to thee: For thou wilt answer me.</p>

<sup>8</sup> There is none like unto thee, O Lord! among the gods; And *there* is none [*who can work*] according to thy works.

<sup>9</sup> All the nations which thou hast made shall come, And shall worship before thy face, O Lord! And glory they shall give to thy name:

<sup>10</sup> For great art thou, And doing wonders, thou God alone.

<sup>11</sup> Show me, O Jehovah! thy ways; I will walk in thy truth: Unite my heart to fear thy name.

<!-- PAGE 958 -->

<sup>12</sup> I will celebrate thee, O Lord my God! with my whole heart; And I will glorify thy name for ever;

<sup>13</sup> For thy mercy hath been great towards me; And thou hast delivered my soul from the lower grave.

<sup>14</sup> O God! the proud have risen up against me, And a company of mighty men have sought after my soul; And they have not set thee before them.

<sup>15</sup> And thou, O Lord! [*art*] a God compassionate and gracious; Slow to anger; and plenteous in mercy and truth.

<sup>16</sup> Look to me, and have pity upon me: Give thy strength to thy servant, And save the son of thy handmaid.

<sup>17</sup> Make with me a sign for good: And see shall my adversaries and be ashamed; For thou, O Jehovah! hast secured me and comforted me.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 87</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*A Psalm [ορ Song of the sons of Korah*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> His foundations [*are*] in the holy mountains.

<sup>2</sup> Love doth Jehovah the gates of Zion More than all the tabernacles of Jacob.

<sup>3</sup> Glorious things are spoken of thee, O city of God! Selah.

<sup>4</sup> I will make mention of Rahab, and Babel among those who know me: Behold the Philistines, and Tyre, with Ethiopia, He is born there! Selah.

<sup>5</sup> And of Zion it shall be said, “Man and man is born in her; “And the Most High himself will establish her.”

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>6</sup> The Lord will recount when he writeth the peoples, “He is born there.” Selah.</p>

<!-- PAGE 959 -->

<sup>7</sup> And the singers as the players upon instruments: All my springs *are* in thee.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 88</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*A Song of a Psalm of the sons of Korah. To the Chief Musician, upon*</span> <span style="color:#000080">*Machalath, to make humble. An instruction of Heman the Ezrahite*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> O Jehovah! God of my salvation! By day I cry, by night before thee.

<sup>2</sup> Come into thy presence let my prayer; Incline thy ear to my cry;

<sup>3</sup> For filled with troubles is my soul; And my life to hell  <span style="color:#800000">ff106</span> hath drawn near.

<sup>4</sup> I am counted with those who go down to the sepulcher, <span style="color:#800000">ff107</span> I have been as a man who hath no strength:

<sup>5</sup> Free among the dead, As the slain lying in the grave, Whom thou rememberest no more, And who from thy hand are cut off.

<sup>6</sup> Thou hast laid me in the lowest pit, In thick darkness, in the deeps.

<sup>7</sup> Upon me lieth heavy thy indignation; And with all thy waves thou hast afflicted me. Selah.

<sup>8</sup> Thou hast removed my acquaintances from me: Thou hast made me abominations to them: I am shut up that I cannot go forth.

<sup>9</sup> My eye mourneth because of my affliction; I invoke thee, O Jehovah! daily: I stretch out to thee my hands.

<sup>10</sup> Wilt thou for the dead do a miracle? Shall the dead rising up praise thee? Selah.

<sup>11</sup> Shall thy loving-kindness be declared in the grave? Thy truth in destruction?

<!-- PAGE 960 -->

<sup>12</sup> Shall thy wonders be known in darkness? And thy righteousness in the land of forgetfulness?

<sup>13</sup> But as for me to thee, O Jehovah! have I cried, And in the morning my prayer shall prevent thee.

<sup>14</sup> Wherefore, O Jehovah! wilt thou reject my soul? [*Wherefore*] wilt thou hide thy face from me?

<sup>15</sup> Afflicted am I, and ready to die from my youth; I have suffered thy terrors by doubting.

<sup>16</sup> Passed over me hath thy fierce wrath: Thy terrors have cut me off.

<sup>17</sup> They have encompassed me as waters daily; They have surrounded me together.

<sup>18</sup> Thou hast removed from me lover and companion; And my acquaintances are darkness.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 89</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*An Instruction of Ethan, the Ezrahite*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> The mercies of Jehovah for ever will I sing: From generation to generation will I celebrate thy truth with my mouth.

<sup>2</sup> For I have said, For ever Mercy shall be built up: The heavens thou shalt establish; thy truth [*is*] in them.

<sup>3</sup> “I have made a covenant with my chosen: “I have sworn to David my servant:

<sup>4</sup> “For ever will I establish thy seed, “And I will build up from age to age thy throne.” Selah.

<sup>5</sup> And celebrate shall the heavens thy wondrous work, O Jehovah! Also thy truth in the congregation of the saints.

<sup>6</sup> For who in the clouds can be compared to Jehovah? Who is like unto Jehovah among the sons of the gods?

<!-- PAGE 961 -->

<sup>7</sup> God is terrible exceedingly in the assembly of the saints, And to be feared above all who are around him.

<sup>8</sup> O Jehovah, God of armies! who *is* a strong God like thee? And thy truth is round about thee.

<sup>9</sup> Thou governest the pride of the sea: When rise up do the waves thereof, thou restrainest them.

<sup>10</sup> Thou hast overthrown Egypt as a wounded mail; With the arm of thy strength thou hast scattered thy enemies.

<sup>11</sup> Thine are the heavens, thine also is the earth: The world, and the fullness thereof thou hast formed. <span style="color:#800000">ff108</span> <sup>12</sup> The north and the south thou hast created: Tabor and Hermon in thy name shall exult.

<sup>13</sup> Thou hast an arm of might: Thou wilt strengthen thy hand, Thou wilt exalt thy right hand.

<sup>14</sup> Righteousness and judgment *are* the seat of thy throne: Mercy and truth shall go before thy face.

<sup>15</sup> Blessed is the people that know the joyful sound! O Jehovah! in the brightness of thy countenance they shall walk.

<sup>16</sup> In thy name shall they exult daily; And in thy righteousness shall they glory:

<sup>17</sup> For the glory of their strength art thou; And in thy favor exalted shall be our horn.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>18</sup> For to Jehovah *is* our buckler; And to. the Holy One of Israel *is* our King.</p>

<sup>19</sup> Then thou spakest in vision to thy meek ones, And saidst, “I have laid help upon a Mighty One; “I have exalted one chosen from among the people.

<sup>20</sup> “I have found David my servant; “With the oil of my holiness have I anointed him.

<!-- PAGE 962 -->

<sup>21</sup> “Therefore, my hand shall be established with him: “Yea, my arm shall strengthen him.

<sup>22</sup> “The enemy shall not exact upon him, “Nor shall the son of iniquity afflict him.

<sup>23</sup> “And I will break in pieces before his face his oppressors; “And those who hate him I will smite.

<sup>24</sup> “And my truth and my mercy *shall be* with him: “And in my name exalted shall be his horn.

<sup>25</sup> “And I will set in the sea his hand, “And in the rivers his right hand.

<sup>26</sup> “He shall cry to me, ‘My Father art thou,  “‘My God, the Rock of my salvation.’

<sup>27</sup> “Also I myself my firstborn will make him, “Higher than the kings of the earth.

<sup>28</sup> “And for ever will I keep for him my mercy, “And my covenant shall stand firm with him.

<sup>29</sup> “And I will establish for ever his seed, “And his throne as the days of heaven.

<sup>30</sup> “If forsake shall his children my law, “And in my judgments shall not walk;

<sup>31</sup> “If my ordinances they shall profane, “And my statutes they shall not keep;

<sup>32</sup> “Then will I visit with my rod their transgressions, “And with stripes their iniquity.

<sup>33</sup> “But my mercy will I not withdraw from him; “Nor will I be false in my truth.

<sup>34</sup> “I will not profane my covenant, “And that which is gone forth from my lips I will not alter.

<!-- PAGE 963 -->
<sup>35</sup> “Once have I sworn by my holiness, “If to David I lie — <sup>36</sup> “His seed for ever shall endure; “And his throne as the sun before me.

<sup>37</sup> “As the moon it shall be established for ever, “And [*as*] a faithful witness in the heaven.” Selah.

<sup>38</sup> But thou, even thou, hast abhorred and rejected *him*; Thou hast been wroth with thy anointed.

<sup>39</sup> Thou hast made to cease the covenant of thy servant; Thou hast profaned to the earth his crown.

<sup>40</sup> Thou hast broken down all his walls; Thou hast made his fortresses a ruin.

<sup>41</sup> Plundered him have all who pass by the way: He hath been a reproach to his neighbors.

<sup>42</sup> Thou hast exalted the right hand of his oppressors; Thou hast made glad all his adversaries.

<sup>43</sup> Also thou hast blunted the edge of his sword, And hast not made him to stand in battle.

<sup>44</sup> Thou hast effaced his splendor, And his throne to the ground thou hast east down.

<sup>45</sup> Thou hast shortened the days of his youth; Thou hast covered him with disgrace. Selah.

<sup>46</sup> How long, O Jehovah! wilt thou hide thyself for ever? Burn like fire shall thy indignation?

<sup>47</sup> Remember how short-lived I am!  <span style="color:#800000">ff109</span> Wherefore in vain shouldst thou have created all the sons of men?

<sup>48</sup> What man shall live, and shall not see death? *And* shall deliver his soul from the hand of the grave? Selah.

<sup>49</sup> Where *are* thy former compassions, O Lord? Thou hast sworn to David in thy truth.

<!-- PAGE 964 -->

<sup>50</sup> Remember, O Lord! the reproach of thy servants: I have sustained in my bosom all *those* [*reproaches*] of the mighty peoples, <sup>51</sup> With which thy enemies have reproached thee, O Jehovah! With which they have reproached the footsteps of thy Messiah.

<sup>52</sup> Blessed *be* Jehovah for evermore. Amen and Amen!

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1">PART FOURTH.<sup>110</sup></p>

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 90</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*A Psalm of Moses, the man of God*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> O Lord! a dwelling-place thou hast been to us from generation to generation.

<sup>2</sup> Before the mountains were brought forth, And [*before*] thou hadst formed the earth and the world, Even from everlasting to everlasting, thou art God.

<sup>3</sup> Thou shalt turn man to destruction, And shalt say, “Return ye sons of Adam.”

<sup>4</sup> For a thousand years in thy sight *are* as yesterday when it is past, And *as* a watch in the night.

<sup>5</sup> Thou overflowest them;  <span style="color:#800000">ff111</span> they will be a sleep: In the morning as grass he shall grow.

<sup>6</sup> In the morning he (*or* it) shall flourish and grow: At the evening he (*or* it) shall be cut down, and shall wither.

<sup>7</sup> For we fail by thy anger, And by thy indignation are we affrighted.

<sup>8</sup> Thou hast set our iniquities before thee, Our secret sins in the light of thy countenance.

<sup>9</sup> For all our days are passed away in thy indignation: We have spent our years as if a thought.

<!-- PAGE 965 -->

<sup>10</sup> In the days of our years *there are* seventy years; And if through strength, eighty years, Yet is their pride [*but*] grief and labor; For it passeth by quickly, and we fly away.

<sup>11</sup> Who knoweth the power of thy anger? And according to thy fear is thy wrath.

<sup>12</sup> Teach us so to number our days, And we shall apply our heart to wisdom.

<sup>13</sup> Return, O Jehovah! how long? Be pacified towards thy servants.

<sup>14</sup> Satiate us early with thy goodness, And we will exult and rejoice all our days.

<sup>15</sup> Make us joyful according to the days of our affliction; *According* to the years in which we have seen evil.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>16</sup> Let thy work appear towards thy servants, And thy glory upon their children.</p>

<sup>17</sup> And let there be the beauty of the Lord our God upon us; And the work of our hands direct thou upon us; Yea, the work of our hands direct thou.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 91</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> He who dwelleth in the secret place of the Most High, Under the shadow of the Almighty shall abide.

<sup>2</sup> I will say to Jehovah, “*Thou art* my hope and my fortress:” *He is* my God; I will hope in him.

<sup>3</sup> Surely he shall deliver thee from the snare of the fowler, From the noxious pestilence:

<sup>4</sup> With his wings he shall protect thee; And under his feathers thou shalt be safe; *Thy* shield and *thy* buckler *shall be* his truth.

<!-- PAGE 966 -->

<sup>5</sup> Thou shalt not be afraid at the terror of the night; At the arrow which flieth by day;

<sup>6</sup> At the destruction which in darkness walketh: At the pestilence which wasteth at noon-day.

<sup>7</sup> There shall fall at thy side a thousand, And ten thousand at thy right hand: To thee it shall not come nigh.

<sup>8</sup> Only with thine eyes shalt thou behold, And the reward of the ungodly thou shalt see.

<sup>9</sup> Because thou, O Jehovah! *art* my protection; The Most High thou hast made thy refuge.

<sup>10</sup> There shall not befall thee [*any*] evil, And the plague shall not come nigh thy tabernacle:

<sup>11</sup> For to his angels he hath given charge concerning thee, To keep thee in all thy ways.

<sup>12</sup> Upon their hands they shall bear thee, Lest thou dash against a stone thy foot.

<sup>13</sup> Over the lion and asp thou shalt walk; Thou shalt tread upon the young lion and dragon.

<sup>14</sup> Because on me he hath reposed, I will deliver him; I will exalt him because he hath known my name.

<sup>15</sup> He will call upon me, and I will answer him: With him will I be in trouble; I will deliver him, and will glorify him.

<sup>16</sup> With length of days will I satisfy him, And will show him my salvation.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 92</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> It is good to give thanks to Jehovah, To sing psalms to thy name, O Most High!

<!-- PAGE 967 -->

<sup>2</sup> To proclaim in the morning thy goodness, And thy truth in the night.

<sup>3</sup> Upon the psaltery, and upon the nablum,  <span style="color:#800000">ff112</span> Upon higgaion with the harp. <span style="color:#800000">ff113</span> <sup>4</sup> For thou hast made me glad, O Jehovah! in thy works; In the works of thy hands I will exult.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>5</sup> How magnificent are thy works, O Jehovah! Exceeding deep are thy thoughts.</p>

<sup>6</sup> The man that is foolish shall not know them, Neither shall he who is void of wisdom understand them.

<sup>7</sup> When the wicked flourish as the grass, And all the workers of iniquity spring up, [*It is*] that they may perish for ever.

<sup>8</sup> But thou *art* exalted for evermore, O Jehovah!

<sup>9</sup> For to! thine enemies, O Jehovah! For to! thine enemies shall perish; Scattered shall be all the workers of iniquity:

<sup>10</sup> But thou shalt exalt my horn like *that* of the unicorn. I am anointed with fresh oil.

<sup>11</sup> And mine eye shall see [*ruin*] on my oppressors: [*Of destruction*,] on those who rise up against me — On those who trouble me hear shall my ears.

<sup>12</sup> The righteous as the palmtree shall flourish, As the cedar in Lebanon he shall be multiplied.

<sup>13</sup> Planted in the house of Jehovah, In the courts of our God, they shall flourish.

<sup>14</sup> Still shall they bud forth in old age; Fat and green shall they be;

<sup>15</sup> That they may proclaim that upright is Jehovah, my Rock, And *that* there is no iniquity in him.

<!-- PAGE 968 -->

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 93</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Jehovah hath reigned, He hath put on glory, Put on hath Jehovah strength; He hath girded himself: Also he hath established the world, It shall not be moved.

<sup>2</sup> Fixed is thy throne;  [*Even as*] from that time  <span style="color:#800000">ff114</span> thou art, and from eternity. <span style="color:#800000">ff115</span> <sup>3</sup> Raised have the floods, O Jehovah! Raised have the floods their voice; Raise shall the floods their waves.

<sup>4</sup> By reason of the voices  <span style="color:#800000">ff116</span> of the great waters Terrible are the waves of the sea: Terrible on high is Jehovah.

<sup>5</sup> Thy testimonies are verified exceedingly; To thy house *is* glory; The holiness of Jehovah *is* for length of days.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 94</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> O Jehovah! God of vengeances! O God of vengeances! shine forth.

<sup>2</sup> Lift up thyself; O judge of the earth! Render a reward to the proud.

<sup>3</sup> How long shall the ungodly, O Jehovah! How long shall the ungodly triumph?

<sup>4</sup> They pour forth, they speak hard things, Extol themselves do all the workers of iniquity.

<sup>5</sup> Thy people, O Jehovah! they break in pieces; And thy inheritance they afflict:

<sup>6</sup> The widow, and the stranger they slay, And the orphans they put to death.

<sup>7</sup> And they have said, “God shall not see, “The God of Jacob shall not know..”

<!-- PAGE 969 -->

<sup>8</sup> Understand, ye stupid among the people! And ye fools! when will ye be wise?

<sup>9</sup> He who planted the ear shall not He hear? He who formed the eye, shall not He see?

<sup>10</sup> He who chastiseth the nations, shall not He correct? He who teacheth man knowledge?

<sup>11</sup> Jehovah knoweth the thoughts of men that they are vain.

<sup>12</sup> Blessed *is* the man whom thou hast instructed, O God! And out of thy law hast taught;

<sup>13</sup> To give him rest from days of evil, Whilst there is digged for the ungodly [*man*] a pit.

<sup>14</sup> Surely Jehovah will not cast off his people, And his inheritance he will not forsake.

<sup>15</sup> For return shall judgment unto righteousness, And [*follow*] after him shall all the upright in heart.

<sup>16</sup> Who will rise up for me against my adversaries? Who will stand up for me against the workers of iniquity?

<sup>17</sup> Unless Jehovah had been a help to me, My soul had well-nigh dwelt in silence.

<sup>18</sup> If I said, “Slidden hath my foot,” Thy goodness, O Jehovah! hath sustained me.

<sup>19</sup> In the multitude of my thoughts, Within me thy comforts cheer my soul.

<sup>20</sup> Shall the throne of iniquities have fellowship with thee, Framing molestation for law?

<sup>21</sup> They will gather together against the soul of the righteous, And the blood of the innocent they will condemn.

<sup>22</sup> But Jehovah hath been to me for a fortress; And my God for the rock of my confidence:

<!-- PAGE 970 -->

<sup>23</sup> And he shall retribute upon them their own iniquity, And in their wickedness he shall destroy them; Destroy them shall Jehovah our God.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 95</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Come let us exult to Jehovah; Let us shout for joy to the Rock of our salvation.

<sup>2</sup> Let us come before his face with praise, In psalms let us shout for joy unto him.

<sup>3</sup> For a great God *is* Jehovah, And a great King, above all gods.

<sup>4</sup> For in his hand *are* the deep places of the earth; And the heights of the mountains are his.

<sup>5</sup> For his is the sea, and he made it; And the dry land his hands formed.

<sup>6</sup> Come ye, let us worship, and fall down; Let us kneel before the face of Jehovah, our Maker.

<sup>7</sup> For he *is* our God, And we [*are*] the people of his pastures, And the flock of his hand; To-day, if his voice ye will hear, <sup>8</sup> Harden not your heart, as in Meribah; As in the day of Massah in the desert,

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>9</sup> When tempt me did your fathers; They proved me, though they had seen my work.</p>

<sup>10</sup> Forty years I strove with this generation, And said, “They are a people erring in heart, “And they have not known my ways.”

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>11</sup> Wherefore I swear in my wrath, “If they shall enter into my rest —”</p>

<!-- PAGE 971 -->

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 96</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Sing ye to Jehovah a song that is new, Sing to Jehovah all the earth.

<sup>2</sup> Sing to Jehovah, bless his name; Publish from day to day his salvation.

<sup>3</sup> Tell among the heathen his glory; Among all the peoples his wonders.

<sup>4</sup> For great *is* Jehovah, and to be praised highly; Terrible [*is*] he above all gods.

<sup>5</sup> For all the gods of the nations are vanities; But Jehovah made the heavens.

<sup>6</sup> Strength and majesty *go* before him; Power and glory *are* in his sanctuary.

<sup>7</sup> Ascribe to Jehovah, O ye congregations of peoples! Ascribe to Jehovah glory and strength.

<sup>8</sup> Ascribe to Jehovah the glory of his name; Bring an offering, and enter into his courts.

<sup>9</sup> Worship before Jehovah in the beauty of the sanctuary; Tremble before his face let the whole earth.

<sup>10</sup> Say ye among the heathen, “Jehovah reigneth; “Truly established shall be the world, it shall not be moved: “He shall judge the peoples in righteousness.”

<sup>11</sup> Rejoice let the heavens, and exult let the earth; Thunder let the sea, and the fullness thereof.

<sup>12</sup> Exult let the field, and all that is in it; Shout for joy together let all the trees of the wood <sup>13</sup> Before Jehovah; for he cometh, For he cometh to judge the earth: He shall judge the world in righteousness, And the peoples in his truth.

<!-- PAGE 972 -->

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 97</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Jehovah reigneth: Exult let the earth, Rejoice let the great islands.

<sup>2</sup> Clouds and darkness *are* round about him; Righteousness and judgment *are* the seat of his throne.

<sup>3</sup> A fire before his face shall go, And shall burn up round about his enemies.

<sup>4</sup> Enlighten did his lightnings the world; See and tremble shall the earth.

<sup>5</sup> The mountains like wax melted at the presence of Jehovah, At the presence of the Lord of the whole earth.

<sup>6</sup> Declared have the heavens his righteousness, And seen have all the peoples his glory.

<sup>7</sup> Confounded be all those who serve graven images, Who glory in *their* inventions: Worship before him let all the gods.

<sup>8</sup> Heard and rejoiced hath Zion; Exulted have the daughters of Judah, Because of thy judgments, O Jehovah!

<sup>9</sup> For thou, O Jehovah! *art* high above all the earth: Exceedingly exalted art thou above all gods.

<sup>10</sup> Ye who love Jehovah, hate evil: He preserveth the souls of his meek ones; From the hand of the ungodly he will deliver them.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>11</sup> Light is sown for the righteous, And for the upright in heart joy.</p>

<sup>12</sup> Rejoice, O ye righteous! in Jehovah, And celebrate the memory of his holiness.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 98</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*A Psalm*</span></p>

<!-- PAGE 973 -->

<sup>1</sup> Sing ye to Jehovah a song that is new, For marvels he hath done: Salvation wrought for him hath his own right hand, And the arm of his holiness.

<sup>2</sup> Made known hath Jehovah his salvation: In the sight of the heathen he hath revealed his righteousness.

<sup>3</sup> He hath remembered his goodness and *his* truth towards the house of Israel: Seen have all the ends of the earth the salvation of our God.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>4</sup> Exult to Jehovah all the earth; Make a loud noise, and exult, and sing psalms.</p>

<sup>5</sup> Sing psalms to Jehovah upon the harp, Upon the harp, and with the voice of song:

<sup>6</sup> With trumpets, and the voice of the cornet, Sing psalms before the King, Jehovah.

<sup>7</sup> Roar let the sea, and the fullness thereof; The world, and those who dwell therein.

<sup>8</sup> Let the floods clap the hand: Together let the mountains exult <sup>9</sup> Before Jehovah: for he cometh to judge the earth; He shall judge the world in righteousness, And the peoples with rectitude.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 99</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Jehovah reigneth; tremble let the people: He dwelleth *between* the cherubim; moved let be the earth.

<sup>2</sup> Jehovah in Zion is great; And exalted is he above all nations.

<sup>3</sup> They shall acknowledge thy great and terrible name: It is holy.

<sup>4</sup> And the strength of the king loveth judgment; Thou hast established rectitude; Judgement and righteousness in Jacob thou hast done.

<!-- PAGE 974 -->

<sup>5</sup> Exalt ye Jehovah our God, And worship at his footstool: He is holy.

<sup>6</sup> Moses and Aaron among his priests, And Samuel among those who call upon his name; They cried to Jehovah and he answered them.

<sup>7</sup> In the pillar of the cloud he spake to them: They kept his testimonies And his statutes which he gave them.

<sup>8</sup> O Jehovah, our God! thou didst answer them: A God propitious thou wast to them, And [*yet*] taking vengeance upon their works.

<sup>9</sup> Exalt ye Jehovah our God, And worship at the mountain of his holiness; For holy *is* Jehovah our God.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 100</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*A Psalm for praising*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Sing aloud to Jehovah, <span style="color:#800000">ff117</span> all ye *inhabitants* of the earth.

<sup>2</sup> Serve Jehovah with gladness; Come into his presence with exultation.

<sup>3</sup> Know that Jehovah He is God; He made us, and not we ourselves: *We are* his people, and the flock of his pasture.

<sup>4</sup> Enter his gates with praise, his courts with exultation; Celebrate him, <span style="color:#800000">ff118</span> bless his name:

<sup>5</sup> For good is Jehovah, For ever *endureth* his mercy, And from generation to generation his truth.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 101</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*A Psalm of David*</span></p>

<!-- PAGE 975 -->

<sup>1</sup> Of mercy and judgment I will sing; To thee, O Jehovah, will I sing psalms.

<sup>2</sup> I will act wisely in a perfect way, Until thou comest to me; I will walk in the integrity of my heart in the midst of my house.

<sup>3</sup> I will not set before my eyes a wicked thing; The work of those who turn aside I hate; It shall not adhere to me.

<sup>4</sup> A heart perverse shall depart from me; Evil I will not know.

<sup>5</sup> He who slandereth in secret his neighbor, him will I destroy: The lofty in look and the wide in heart him I will not suffer.

<sup>6</sup> My eyes *shall be* upon the faithful of the land, That they may dwell with me: He who walketh in an upright way, [*Even*] he shall minister to me.

<sup>7</sup> He shall not dwell in the midst of my house, he who worketh deceit; He who speaketh lies shall not be established before my eyes.

<sup>8</sup> Early will I destroy all the ungodly of the land; That I may cut off from the city of Jehovah all the workers of iniquity.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 102</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*A Prayer for the afflicted when he shall be shut up, and shall pour out his*</span> <span style="color:#000080">*meditation before Jehovah*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> O Jehovah! hear my prayer, And let my cry to thee come.

<sup>2</sup> Hide not thy face from me in the day of my afflictions; Incline to me thy ear; In the day when I cry, make haste, answer me.

<sup>3</sup> For consumed like smoke are  <span style="color:#800000">ff119</span> my days, And my bones as a hearth are burnt up.

<sup>4</sup> Smitten like grass, and withered is my heart, Because I have forgotten to eat my bread.

<!-- PAGE 976 -->

<sup>5</sup> By reason of the voice of my groaning, Cleave do my bones to my flesh.

<sup>6</sup> I am become like a pelican of the desert, I am become as an owl of the solitudes;

<sup>7</sup> I have watched and am as the sparrow *Which is* solitary upon the roof of the house.

<sup>8</sup> Daily reproach me do my enemies; And those who are mad against me, by me have sworn.

<sup>9</sup> For ashes as bread I have eaten, And my drink with weeping <span style="color:#800000">ff120</span> I have mingled.

<sup>10</sup> Because of thy indignation and thy wrath; For thou hast lifted me up and cast me down.

<sup>11</sup> My days *are* as a shadow which declineth; And as grass I am dried up.

<sup>12</sup> And thou, O Jehovah! for ever shalt dwell, And thy memorial from generation to generation.

<sup>13</sup> Thou thyself shalt arise, thou shalt have mercy upon Zion; For it is time to compassionate her, For come is the appointed time.

<sup>14</sup> For take pleasure do thy servants in her stones, And upon her dust they will have compassion.

<sup>15</sup> And fear shall the Gentiles the name of Jehovah, And all the kings of the earth thy glory.

<sup>16</sup> For Jehovah hath builded Zion, He hath appeared in his glory.

<sup>17</sup> He hath regarded the prayer of the solitary, And hath not despised their prayer.

<sup>18</sup> This shall be written for the generation to come, And the people [*to be*] created shall praise him.

<!-- PAGE 977 -->
<sup>19</sup> For he hath looked from the high *place* of his holiness, Jehovah from the heavens upon the earth hath looked, <sup>20</sup> To hear the groaning of the prisoners To release the sons of death;

<sup>21</sup> That declared in Zion may be the name of Jehovah, And his praise in Jerusalem;

<sup>22</sup> When gathered shall be the peoples together, And the kingdoms to serve Jehovah.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>23</sup> He hath afflicted in the way my strength, He hath shortened my days.</p>

<sup>24</sup> I said, O my God! take me not away in the midst of my days; *For* throughout generations of generations *are* thy years.

<sup>25</sup> Aforetime the earth thou hast founded; And the work of thy hands serve the heavens.

<sup>26</sup> They shall perish but thou shalt endure; And they all as a garment shall grow old; As a vesture thou shalt change them, and they shall be changed.

<sup>27</sup> But thou *art always* the same, And thy years shall not fail.

<sup>28</sup> The children of thy servants shall dwell, And their seed before thee shall be established.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 103</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*Of David*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Bless, O my soul! Jehovah, And all my inward parts *bless* his holy name.

<sup>2</sup> Bless, O my soul! Jehovah, And forget not all his bounties.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>3</sup> Who forgiveth all thy iniquities; Who healeth all thy infirmities;</p>

<sup>4</sup> Who redeemeth from the grave thy life; Who crowneth thee with mercy and compassions;

<!-- PAGE 978 -->

<sup>5</sup> Who satisfieth with good thy mouth: Renewed as *that* of the eagle shall be thy youth.

<sup>6</sup> Execute doth Jehovah righteousness And judgments for all who are oppressed.

<sup>7</sup> He made known his ways to Moses, To the children of Israel his doings.

<sup>8</sup> Compassionate and merciful *is* Jehovah, Slow to anger and abundant in goodness.

<sup>9</sup> He will not always chide; Neither for ever will he keep [h*is anger*.] <sup>10</sup> Not according to our sins hath he dealt with us, Nor according to our iniquities hath he recompensed us.

<sup>11</sup> For in proportion to the height of the heavens above the earth, Hath been the greatness of his goodness upon those who fear him.

<sup>12</sup> As far distant as is the east from the west, So far hath he removed from us our sills.

<sup>13</sup> As a father is compassionate towards his children, So is Jehovah compassionate towards those who fear him.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>14</sup> For he knoweth of what we are made; He hath remembered that dust are we.</p>

<sup>15</sup> Man… as grass *are* his days: As a flower of the field, so he flourisheth.

<sup>16</sup> As soon as the wind passeth over it, it is not; And know it no more shall the place thereof.

<sup>17</sup> But the goodness of Jehovah *is* from everlasting to everlasting upon those who fear him, And his righteousness upon the children’s children;

<sup>18</sup> To those who keep his covenant, And are mindful of his statutes to do them.

<!-- PAGE 979 -->

<sup>19</sup> Jehovah in the heavens hath established his throne; And his kingdom over all hath dominion.

<sup>20</sup> Bless Jehovah, *ye* his angels mighty in strength! Who do his commandment, in hearing the voice of his word.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>21</sup> Bless Jehovah, all *ye* his armies! Ye ministers of his, who do his pleasure.</p>

<sup>22</sup> Bless Jehovah, all ye his works! In all places of his dominion: Bless, O my soul! Jehovah.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 104</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Bless, O my soul! Jehovah: O Jehovah, my God! thou art great exceedingly; With praise and honor thou hast clothed thyself.

<sup>2</sup> Being arrayed with light as with a garment; Spreading out the heavens as a curtain;

<sup>3</sup> Flooring in the waters his upper rooms; Making the clouds his chariot; Walking upon the wings of the wind:

<sup>4</sup> Making the winds his messengers; His ministers a fire that flameth.

<sup>5</sup> He hath founded the earth upon its foundations, So that it shall not be moved for ever.

<sup>6</sup> With the deep as with a garment he hath covered it: Above the mountains stand shall the waters.

<sup>7</sup> At thy rebuke they shall flee; At the voice of thy thunder they shall suddenly pass away.

<sup>8</sup> Ascend shall the mountains and descend shall the valleys To the place which thou hast founded for them.

<sup>9</sup> A boundary thou hast set, they shall not pass over *it*: They shall not return to cover the earth.

<!-- PAGE 980 -->

<sup>10</sup> Sending out springs by the valleys, *Which* between the hills shall run.

<sup>11</sup> Drink *thereof* shall all the beasts of the field: Quench shall the wild asses their thirst.

<sup>12</sup> Nigh time the fowl of the heaven shall dwell, From the midst of the branches they shall send out their voice.

<sup>13</sup> Watering the mountains from his chambers: From the fruit of thy works satisfied shall be the earth.

<sup>14</sup> Making grass to grow for cattle, And herb for the service of mm: That he may bring forth bread out of the earth.

<sup>15</sup> And wine cheereth the heart of man, To make *his* face to shine with oil, And bread sustaineth the heart of man.

<sup>16</sup> Satiated shall be the trees of Jehovah; The cedars of Lebanon which he hath planted:

<sup>17</sup> For there the birds build their nests: [*As for*] the stork, the fir trees are her dwelling.

<sup>18</sup> The high mountains *are* for the deer; The rocks a place of shelter for the hedgehogs.

<sup>19</sup> He hath made the moon for set times: The sun knoweth his going down.

<sup>20</sup> Thou makest darkness, and it is night; Wherein do creep forth all the beasts of the forest.

<sup>21</sup> The lions roar after their prey, And in seeking from God their food.

<sup>22</sup> Rise shall the sun, and they shall gather themselves together, And in their dens shall lie down.

<sup>23</sup> Go forth shall man to his work, And to his labor, until the evening.

<!-- PAGE 981 -->

<sup>24</sup> How magnificent are thy works! O Jehovah! All things in wisdom thou hast made: Filled is the earth with thy riches.

<sup>25</sup> This sea great, and wide in extent; Therein are creeping things inntanerable, Beasts both small and great.

<sup>26</sup> There the ships go, And the leviathan, which thou hast formed to sport in it.

<sup>27</sup> All things on thee do wait, That thou mayest give *them* their food in its season.

<sup>28</sup> Thou shalt give it them, they shall gather [*it*:] Thou shalt open thy hand, they shall be satisfied with good.

<sup>29</sup> Thou shalt hide thy face, they shall be afraid: Thou shalt take away their spirit, they shall die, and to their dust shall return.

<sup>30</sup> Thou shalt send forth thy Spirit, they shall be created: And thou shalt renew the face of the earth.

<sup>31</sup> Glory be to Jehovah for ever: Rejoice let Jehovah in his works.

<sup>32</sup> He looketh upon the earth, and it trembleth: If he touch the mountains, they shall smoke.

<sup>33</sup> I will sing to Jehovah whilst I live: I will sing psalms to my God as long as I have my being.

<sup>34</sup> Let my speech be acceptable to him: I will rejoice in Jehovah.

<sup>35</sup> Perish let sinners from the earth, And the ungodly till they cease to be any more. Bless, O my soul! Jehovah. Hallelujah.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 105</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Celebrate ye Jehovah; invoke his name: Announce among the peoples his works.

<sup>2</sup> Sing ye to him: sing psalms to him: Speak of all his wonders.

<!-- PAGE 982 -->

<sup>3</sup> Rejoice ye in his holy name: Rejoice let the heart of those who seek Jehovah.

<sup>4</sup> Seek ye Jehovah, and his strength; Seek his face continually.

<sup>5</sup> Remember the marvelous works which he hath done; His wonders, and the judgments of his mouth.

<sup>6</sup> *Ye* seed of Abraham, his servant, The sons of Jacob his chosen one:

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>7</sup> He *is* Jehovah our God; Through all the earth are his judgments.</p>

<sup>8</sup> He hath remembered for ever his covenant, The word *which* he commanded to a thousand generations:

<sup>9</sup> Which he made with Abraham, And his oath *which he swore* to Isaac;

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>10</sup> And established it to Jacob for a law, To Israel for an everlasting covenant:</p>

<sup>11</sup> Saying, “To thee will I give the land of Canaan, “The cord of your inheritance.”

<sup>12</sup> When they were very few in number; And strangers in it;

<sup>13</sup> And walked about from nation to nation, From *one* kingdom to another people, <sup>14</sup> He did not suffer men to hurt them; And rebuked for their sake kings;

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>15</sup> *Saying*, “Touch not my anointed ones, “And to my prophets do no harm.”</p>

<sup>16</sup> And he called a famine upon the land; All the stall of bread he broke.

<sup>17</sup> He sent before them a man, For a slave sold was Joseph.

<sup>18</sup> They afflicted in the fetters his feet: The iron entered into his soul:

<!-- PAGE 983 -->

<sup>19</sup> Until the time that his word came: The word of Jehovah tried him.

<sup>20</sup> Send did the king and loosed him; Even the ruler of the peoples, and freed him.

<sup>21</sup> He made him lord over his house; And ruler over all his substance:

<sup>22</sup> To bind his princes at his pleasure; And to teach his elders wisdom.

<sup>23</sup> And come did Israel into Egypt; And Jacob sojourned in the land of Ham.

<sup>24</sup> And he increased his people exceedingly, He multiplied them above their oppressors.

<sup>25</sup> He turned their heart so that they hated his people, And dealt craftily with his servants.

<sup>26</sup> He sent Moses, his servant; Aaron, whom he chose.

<sup>27</sup> They set among them the words of his signs, And *his* miracles in the land of Ham.

<sup>28</sup> He sent darkness, and made it dark; And they were not rebellious against his words.

<sup>29</sup> He turned their waters into blood, And killed their fish.

<sup>30</sup> Their land brought forth frogs, *Yea even* within the chambers of their kings.

<sup>31</sup> He spake and there came a swarm of flies, Lice in all their borders.

<sup>32</sup> He gave them for rain, hail, Flaming fire upon their land.

<sup>33</sup> And he smote their vines and their fig-trees; And destroyed *every* tree of their border.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>34</sup> He spake, and there came the locust, And the caterpillar without number;</p>

<!-- PAGE 984 -->

<sup>35</sup> And they devoured all the herbage in their land, And consumed the fruit of their ground.

<sup>36</sup> And he smote all the firstborn in their land, The beginning of all their strength.

<sup>37</sup> And he brought them forth with silver and gold: And not one among their tribes was a feeble person.

<sup>38</sup> Glad was Egypt at their departure; For fallen upon them had their terror.

<sup>39</sup> He spread out a cloud for a covering; And fire to give light by the night.

<sup>40</sup> He  <span style="color:#800000">ff121</span> asked, and brought quails, And with the bread of heaven he filled them.

<sup>41</sup> He opened the rock, and there gushed out waters; They ran in the dry *places like* a river.

<sup>42</sup> For he remembered the word of his holiness, *Which he had spoken* to Abraham his servant.

<sup>43</sup> And he brought forth his people with joy, With exultation his chosen ones.

<sup>44</sup> And he gave them the lands of the nations: And the labor of the peoples they inherited.

<sup>45</sup> That they might keep his ordinances, And that his law they might observe. Hallelujah.

<p class="title-block-h2" style="text-align:center; font-size:16px; font-weight:bold; margin:18px 0 12px;" markdown="1"><span style="color:#006411">PSALM 106</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center; color:#000080; margin:14px 2em;"><span style="color:#000080">*Hallelujah*</span></p>

<sup>1</sup> Celebrate Jehovah, for he is good; For to everlasting *is* his mercy.

<sup>2</sup> Who shall express the mighty acts of Jehovah? Who shall declare all his praise?

<!-- PAGE 985 -->

<sup>3</sup> Blessed are they who keep judgment, *Blessed* is he who doeth righteousness at all times.

<sup>4</sup> Remember me, O Jehovah! with the good will [*which thou bearest towards*] thy people. Visit me with thy salvation;

<sup>5</sup> That I may see the good of thy chosen ones, That I may rejoice in the joy of thy nation, That I may glory with thy heritage.

<sup>6</sup> We have sinned with our fathers, We have acted iniquitously, we have done wickedly.

<sup>7</sup> Our fathers in Egypt understood not thy wonders; They remembered not the multitude of thy kindnesses; And they rebelled at the sea, at the Red Sea.

<sup>8</sup> Yet he saved them for the sake of his own name, That. he might cause to be known his power.

<sup>9</sup> And he rebuked the Red Sea, and it was dried up; And he made them walk in the deeps as in a desert.

<sup>10</sup> And he saved them from the hand of the enemy, And redeemed them from the hand of the adversary.

<sup>11</sup> And cover did the waters their oppressors: One of them was not left.

<sup>12</sup> Then they believed his words; They sang his praise.

<sup>13</sup> They made haste, they forgat his works; They did not wait for his counsel;

<sup>14</sup> And they lusted greatly in the desert, And tempted God in the wilderness.

<sup>15</sup> And he gave them their request; But sent leanness into their souls.

<sup>16</sup> And they envied Moses in the camp, And Aaron, the saint of Jehovah.

<!-- PAGE 986 -->

<sup>17</sup> Open did the earth and swallowed up Dathan, And covered over the tent of Abiram.

<sup>18</sup> And kindled was a fire in their company, The flame burned up the ungodly.

<sup>19</sup> They made a calf in Horeb, And worshipped before the molten image.

<sup>20</sup> And they changed their glory Into the likeness of an ox that eateth hay.

<sup>21</sup> They forgat God their Savior, Who had done great things in Egypt;

<sup>22</sup> Miracles in the land of Ham, Terrible things at the Red Sea.

<sup>23</sup> And he said that he would destroy them, Unless Moses his chosen had stood in the breach before his face, To turn away his wrath, that he might not destroy *them*.

<sup>24</sup> And they despised the land of pleasantness; They believed not his word;

<sup>25</sup> And they mutinied in their tents, They hearkened not to the voice of Jehovah.

<sup>26</sup> And he lifted tip his hand against them, To destroy them in the desert:

<sup>27</sup> And to scatter their seed among the heathen, And to disperse them throughout the lands.
